# REVIEW-057 round three — the redesigned guard, the rebuilt inventory, and step 7

This is the third and last round on the phase 1 publication plan. Round two found
12 defects, four HIGH, and the response was not a set of patches: the push guard was
redesigned and the third-party inventory rebuilt. **Neither has been reviewed by
anyone.** Your job is to break them, to find a round-two fix that does not discharge
its finding, and to find anything in the plan that would still leak, publish a false
statement, or fail on publication day.

This repository's gates have false-passed many times, including twice in this
rotation: the guard's first self-test reported every mutant caught while 17 cases
already failed, and the first inventory check passed a `NOTICE` of bare names with a
licence file deleted. Treat every PASS you are shown as a claim to falsify.

## STEP 0 — do this before anything else, and report the results first

1. Run `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://github.com` and
   `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://registry.npmjs.org`. Print
   both codes. Run `gh auth status 2>&1 | head -3`. If network fails, print
   `NO NETWORK` and continue without it.
2. Run `pwd` and `git log --oneline -1`. The working copy is a clone at commit
   `a36f165`. Work only inside this clone.
3. **Do not read** any directory outside this clone, and in particular: any path
   under `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.wconf`, `/tmp/wconf`, any `scratchpad`
   directory, and the repository this clone was made from. `~/.wconf` and
   `/tmp/wconf` hold real (Devnet, no-value) secrets. **Never print, cat or copy
   them.** Do not run `make check-no-secrets` without `--no-local-store`, and do
   not run `cargo`, `make check-zeroize-bounds` or `tools/rebuild-env.sh`.
   **One exception**: `~/.cargo/registry/src` may be read, and only read, because
   the inventory compares the signer's locked crates from there.
4. You may write scratch files under `$TMPDIR`. Do not commit anything. **Never
   run `git push` to a network URL, including `--dry-run`**: test the guard
   against local bare repositories only. Do not create, rename or edit anything on
   GitHub. Read-only `gh` calls only.
5. ⚠ **Your transcript is committed to this repository after redaction.** Three
   sources must never appear in it as text:
   - **The private repository's issue bodies and comments.** You may query
     `nandanito/xrpl-confidential-positions` only through `--json … --jq` that
     prints numbers, states, dates, counts, titles and `U-nn` ids. Never print a
     body or a comment, or any sentence from one.
   - **Withheld rows.** `docs/upstream-observed-WITHHELD.md` is gitignored and not
     in your clone. Do not try to obtain it, and do not run `git log -p`,
     `git show`, `git blame` or `git diff` on `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` at any commit
     other than `a36f165`.
   - **Third-party source, printed whole.** Round two's transcript printed 150
     lines of `@noble/curves` to plant a probe, and that put a new project into
     this repository's licence notice. **Generate probe material without printing
     it**: write it to a file and print only its length and a hash, or print at
     most two lines of it.

## The four seams. Answer every one, each with an explicit verdict

For each seam list findings as `R3-n | HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW | claim | evidence you ran or
read, with paths and line numbers | the smallest fix`. **If a seam has no finding,
write `Seam N: no findings` and say what you tried.** Grade HIGH only for something
that would leak private material, publish a false statement of fact, or make the
publication fail.

### Seam 1 — did each round-two fix discharge its finding?

R2-1 to R2-12, one at a time, against round two's wording and `REVIEW-057d`'s fix.
**Judge against the class, not the example.** Write `discharged`, `not discharged`
or `discharged in part`, with the reason.

### Seam 2 — the push guard, redesigned

The property is now: a private clone pushes only to the archive, identified by its
repository id on github.com, whatever the push holds. Try to make the hook allow a
push to anywhere else, or refuse a push to the archive, using local bare
repositories and the fake `gh` pattern the self-test uses. Consider at least: URL
forms git accepts; what actually connects, as against what the URL says
(`insteadOf`, `pushurl`, SSH configuration, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `core.sshCommand`,
transport helpers); `gh` configuration and environment beyond `GH_HOST`; the
installer's choices of remote and hooks directory, worktrees, `core.hooksPath`; and
every exit path. **Then attack `tools/check-push-guard.sh`**: write a broken hook
it passes, if you can.

### Seam 3 — the inventory, rebuilt

Run it (`bun run tools/inventory-third-party.ts --check`, network needed). Then find
third-party material it still misses, and wrong attributions it still makes. Think
about what the new rules exclude — numeric-dense windows, manifests, licence text,
the `DERIVED_FROM` rule — and whether each exclusion can hide a real quotation.
**Attack the `NOTICE` manifest check**: can a `NOTICE` that fails its obligations
pass it? Check every statement in `NOTICE` against the inventory and the tree.

### Seam 4 — steps 5 to 10, and what publication day depends on

Walk steps 5 to 10 as the person executing them. Does anything the plan relies on
go unchecked on the exact commit that is pushed? In particular: is the inventory
re-run on the export, and is the README sentence's placeholder filled before the
gate? Is step 7 executable with the local store as written, and is D7's issue
procedure executable as written? Report what you rehearsed and what you did not.

## The report

End with: a table of every finding; one line per seam saying whether it survives;
and **the single change you would make before step 3 runs**.

/bin/zsh -lc 'nl -ba tools/inventory-third-party.ts' in $USER_TMP/T/review057r3.NMFyrF/repo
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * The third-party inventory: which tracked files reproduce upstream source or
     3	 * specification text, from which project, under which licence.
     4	 *
     5	 * Decision request phase 1, D3 and item 5 step 1 (`REVIEW-057` R1-1). The
     6	 * `NOTICE` is written from this tool's output, not from memory: the first
     7	 * draft named one patch and one harness, and the records quoted a whole
     8	 * BSD-3-Clause file nobody had counted.
     9	 *
    10	 * Method. Each upstream source is a CORPUS fetched at a stated version. A
    11	 * tracked file reproduces a corpus where:
    12	 *   - CODE: four or more substantive lines in a row (gaps of up to two
    13	 *     non-substantive lines allowed) each appear verbatim, after trimming and
    14	 *     collapsing whitespace, somewhere in that corpus; or
    15	 *   - CODE, across lines: a run of 24-token windows appears in that corpus, so
    16	 *     code joined onto one line or reflowed by a formatter is still found; or
    17	 *   - PROSE (specification and documentation corpora only): a run of 12
    18	 *     consecutive words appears in that corpus, so a quotation that was
    19	 *     re-wrapped or inlined is still found.
    20	 * Before either code pass, a tracked line loses a line-number prefix of the
    21	 * kind `cat -n`, `nl` and `grep -n` print: the first version of this tool
    22	 * matched lines exactly and missed every numbered quotation in the review
    23	 * transcripts (`REVIEW-057` R2-5).
    24	 * A substantive line has at least 20 characters after normalising and at
    25	 * least one letter. The thresholds are a choice, printed in the output.
    26	 *
    27	 *   bun run tools/inventory-third-party.ts            # fetch, compare, write
    28	 *   bun run tools/inventory-third-party.ts --check    # also fail if NOTICE omits a project found
    29	 *
    30	 * Needs network for the GitHub corpora; the crates come from the local cargo
    31	 * registry and the npm packages from node_modules. Not part of `make ci`.
    32	 */
    33	import { spawnSync } from "bun";
    34	import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
    35	import { join, relative } from "node:path";
    36	
    37	const ROOT = join(import.meta.dir, "..");
    38	const CACHE = process.env.WCONF_INVENTORY_CACHE ?? join(process.env.TMPDIR ?? "/tmp", "wconf-third-party-corpora");
    39	const OUT = join(ROOT, "docs/third-party/INVENTORY.md");
    40	const MIN_RUN = 4;
    41	const MAX_GAP = 2;
    42	const MIN_LINE = 20;
    43	const SHINGLE = 12;
    44	const TOKEN_WINDOW = 24;
    45	const MIN_TOKEN_RUN = 12;
    46	
    47	type Corpus = { name: string; version: string; licence: string; dir: string; prose: boolean };
    48	
    49	function run(cmd: string[], cwd?: string): string {
    50	  const r = spawnSync(cmd, { cwd, stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe" });
    51	  if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`${cmd.join(" ")} failed: ${r.stderr.toString().trim()}`);
    52	  return r.stdout.toString();
    53	}
    54	
    55	/** A shallow fetch of one ref of a GitHub repository into the cache. */
    56	function github(repo: string, ref: string): string {
    57	  const dir = join(CACHE, `${repo.replace("/", "__")}@${ref}`);
    58	  if (existsSync(join(dir, ".done"))) return dir;
    59	  mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    60	  run(["git", "init", "-q"], dir);
    61	  run(["git", "fetch", "-q", "--depth", "1", `https://github.com/${repo}.git`, ref], dir);
    62	  run(["git", "-c", "advice.detachedHead=false", "checkout", "-q", "FETCH_HEAD"], dir);
    63	  writeFileSync(join(dir, ".done"), "");
    64	  return dir;
    65	}
    66	
    67	/**
    68	 * Only the files of a repository whose path matches, fetched one by one: the
    69	 * dev portal is far too large to clone for the pages that concern this project.
    70	 */
    71	function pathsMatching(repo: string, ref: string, re: RegExp): Corpus {
    72	  const dir = join(CACHE, `${repo.replace("/", "__")}@${ref}-${re.source}`);
    73	  if (!existsSync(join(dir, ".done"))) {
    74	    mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    75	    const tree = JSON.parse(run(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/git/trees/${ref}?recursive=1`]));
    76	    if (tree.truncated) throw new Error(`${repo} tree listing is truncated; the path filter would miss files`);
    77	    for (const e of tree.tree) {
    78	      if (e.type !== "blob" || !re.test(e.path) || !TEXT.test(e.path)) continue;
    79	      const body = run(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/contents/${encodeURI(e.path)}?ref=${ref}`, "-H", "Accept: application/vnd.github.raw"]);
    80	      const out = join(dir, e.path);
    81	      mkdirSync(join(out, ".."), { recursive: true });
    82	      writeFileSync(out, body);
    83	    }
    84	    writeFileSync(join(dir, ".done"), "");
    85	  }
    86	  // The root licence file, so the NOTICE check can compare LICENSES/ against it.
    87	  if (!existsSync(join(dir, "LICENSE"))) {
    88	    writeFileSync(join(dir, "LICENSE"), run(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/contents/LICENSE?ref=${ref}`, "-H", "Accept: application/vnd.github.raw"]));
    89	  }
    90	  return { name: repo, version: `${ref}, paths matching ${re}`, licence: githubLicence(repo), dir, prose: true };
    91	}
    92	
    93	function githubLicence(repo: string): string {
    94	  const r = spawnSync(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/license`, "--jq", ".license.spdx_id"], { stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe" });
    95	  return r.exitCode === 0 ? r.stdout.toString().trim() : "no licence file found";
    96	}
    97	
    98	function crates(): Corpus[] {
    99	  const lock = readFileSync(join(ROOT, "signer/Cargo.lock"), "utf8");
   100	  const registry = join(process.env.HOME ?? "", ".cargo/registry/src");
   101	  const indexes = existsSync(registry) ? readdirSync(registry).map((d) => join(registry, d)) : [];
   102	  const out: Corpus[] = [];
   103	  for (const m of lock.matchAll(/\[\[package\]\]\nname = "([^"]+)"\nversion = "([^"]+)"\nsource = "registry/g)) {
   104	    const [, name, version] = m;
   105	    const dir = indexes.map((i) => join(i, `${name}-${version}`)).find((d) => existsSync(d));
   106	    if (!dir) {
   107	      out.push({ name: `crate ${name}`, version, licence: "NOT FOUND LOCALLY — not compared", dir: "", prose: false });
   108	      continue;
   109	    }
   110	    const toml = readFileSync(join(dir, "Cargo.toml"), "utf8");
   111	    const licence = toml.match(/^license\s*=\s*"([^"]+)"/m)?.[1] ?? "see crate";
   112	    out.push({ name: `crate ${name}`, version, licence, dir, prose: false });
   113	  }
   114	  return out;
   115	}
   116	
   117	function npmPackages(): Corpus[] {
   118	  const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(ROOT, "package.json"), "utf8"));
   119	  const names = [...Object.keys(pkg.dependencies ?? {}), ...Object.keys(pkg.devDependencies ?? {})];
   120	  return names.map((name) => {
   121	    const dir = join(ROOT, "node_modules", name);
   122	    if (!existsSync(join(dir, "package.json"))) return { name: `npm ${name}`, version: "?", licence: "NOT INSTALLED — not compared", dir: "", prose: false };
   123	    const p = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, "package.json"), "utf8"));
   124	    return { name: `npm ${name}`, version: p.version, licence: p.license ?? "see package", dir, prose: false };
   125	  });
   126	}
   127	
   128	function corpora(): Corpus[] {
   129	  const gh = (name: string, ref: string, prose = false): Corpus => ({
   130	    name, version: ref, licence: githubLicence(name), dir: github(name, ref), prose,
   131	  });
   132	  return [
   133	    gh("XRPLF/mpt-crypto", "85dbac73d033241b73f0565ae7cce86d07005c70"),
   134	    gh("XRPLF/mpt-crypto", "main"),
   135	    gh("XRPLF/rippled", "3.3.0"),
   136	    gh("XRPLF/rippled", "develop"),
   137	    gh("XRPLF/clio", "develop"),
   138	    gh("XRPLF/XRPL-Standards", "master", true),
   139	    // The last XLS-0096 revision before PR #604 set it Final; quotations of the
   140	    // older wording come from here. Resolved with `gh api`, not typed.
   141	    gh("XRPLF/XRPL-Standards", "9fbf7ef8eb69973f43d44836875e01e6364a3ecb", true),
   142	    // GitHub reports NOASSERTION for the dev portal, because its LICENSE also lists
   143	    // bundled libraries. The file grants MIT for the portal's original contents
   144	    // (read 2026-10-18).
   145	    { ...pathsMatching("XRPLF/xrpl-dev-portal", "master", /confidential/i), licence: "MIT, per its LICENSE (GitHub reports NOASSERTION)" },
   146	    ...crates(),
   147	    ...npmPackages(),
   148	  ];
   149	}
   150	
   151	const norm = (s: string) => s.trim().replace(/\s+/g, " ");
   152	const substantive = (s: string) => s.length >= MIN_LINE && /[A-Za-z]/.test(s);
   153	const TEXT = /\.(c|h|cc|cpp|hpp|ipp|rs|ts|tsx|js|mjs|cjs|jsx|md|txt|py|sh|toml|json|yml|yaml|cmake|in|patch|html|css)$|(^|\/)(CMakeLists\.txt|Makefile|LICENSE[^/]*)$/i;
   154	
   155	function walk(dir: string, into: string[] = []): string[] {
   156	  for (const e of readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   157	    if (e.name === ".git" || e.name === "node_modules") continue;
   158	    const p = join(dir, e.name);
   159	    if (e.isDirectory()) walk(p, into);
   160	    else if (e.isFile() && TEXT.test(p) && statSync(p).size < 2_000_000) into.push(p);
   161	  }
   162	  return into;
   163	}
   164	
   165	function words(s: string): string[] {
   166	  return s.toLowerCase().replace(/[`*_>#|\[\]()]/g, " ").split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean);
   167	}
   168	
   169	/**
   170	 * A line-number prefix, as `cat -n` and `nl` (`   12\t`), numbered viewers
   171	 * (`12→`, `12|`), and `grep -n` with or without a file name (`src/x.c:12:`,
   172	 * `12:`) print it. Stripping too much can only add matches, which is the safe
   173	 * direction for a notice.
   174	 */
   175	const PREFIXES = [/^\s*\d+\t/, /^\s*\d+\s*[→│|]\s?/, /^[\w.\/+@-]*\.\w+[:-]\d+[:-]/, /^\d+[:-]/];
   176	function stripPrefix(l: string): string {
   177	  for (const re of PREFIXES) if (re.test(l)) return l.replace(re, "");
   178	  return l;
   179	}
   180	
   181	/**
   182	 * Identifiers and numbers only. Punctuation tokens made box-drawing lines and
   183	 * operator-heavy idioms match across projects, and comment markers kept a
   184	 * licence header in a source file from matching the same licence's file.
   185	 */
   186	function tokens(text: string): { tok: string[]; line: number[] } {
   187	  const tok: string[] = [], line: number[] = [];
   188	  text.split("\n").forEach((l, i) => {
   189	    for (const m of l.matchAll(/[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*|\d\w*/g)) { tok.push(m[0]); line.push(i); }
   190	  });
   191	  return { tok, line };
   192	}
   193	
   194	/** A window of mostly names, not numbers: a table of published constants is no quotation. */
   195	function windowValid(tok: string[], i: number): boolean {
   196	  let names = 0;
   197	  for (let k = i; k < i + TOKEN_WINDOW; k++) if (/^[A-Za-z_]/.test(tok[k])) names++;
   198	  return names * 2 >= TOKEN_WINDOW;
   199	}
   200	const MANIFEST = /(^|\/)(Cargo\.toml(\.orig)?|package\.json|[^/]*\.lock)$/;
   201	
   202	/** A 53-bit hash of one token window, from two independent 32-bit hashes. */
   203	function windowHash(tok: string[], i: number): number {
   204	  const s = tok.slice(i, i + TOKEN_WINDOW).join(" ");
   205	  return (Bun.hash.crc32(s) * 0x200000) + (Bun.hash.adler32(s) & 0x1fffff);
   206	}
   207	
   208	/** A project is a repository, crate or package, whatever its version. */
   209	const project = (c: Corpus) => c.name;
   210	
   211	// line -> the projects whose corpus contains it; and the lines of licence files.
   212	const LINES = new Map<string, Set<string>>();
   213	const LICENCE_LINES = new Set<string>();
   214	const SHINGLES = new Map<string, Set<string>>();
   215	const VERSIONS = new Map<string, Set<string>>();
   216	// Token windows: the tracked files' hashes, and for each one found in a corpus
   217	// the projects that hold it. Only tracked hashes are kept, so a corpus is
   218	// streamed rather than stored.
   219	const TRACKED_HASHES = new Set<number>();
   220	const TOKEN_HITS = new Map<number, Set<string>>();
   221	// Windows found in any project's licence file: never evidence of a quotation.
   222	const LICENCE_WINDOWS = new Set<number>();
   223	// Each project's root licence files, for the NOTICE check.
   224	const LICENCE_FILES = new Map<string, Buffer[]>();
   225	
   226	function index(c: Corpus): number {
   227	  let n = 0;
   228	  for (const f of walk(c.dir)) {
   229	    const text = readFileSync(f, "utf8");
   230	    const isLicence = /(^|\/)(LICEN[CS]E|COPYING|NOTICE)[^/]*$/i.test(f);
   231	    for (const l of text.split("\n")) {
   232	      const s = norm(l);
   233	      if (!substantive(s)) continue;
   234	      if (isLicence) LICENCE_LINES.add(s);
   235	      let set = LINES.get(s);
   236	      if (!set) LINES.set(s, (set = new Set()));
   237	      set.add(project(c));
   238	      n++;
   239	    }
   240	    if (isLicence && !relative(c.dir, f).includes("/")) {
   241	      if (!LICENCE_FILES.has(project(c))) LICENCE_FILES.set(project(c), []);
   242	      LICENCE_FILES.get(project(c))!.push(readFileSync(f));
   243	    }
   244	    // Licence texts are left to the line pass, which classifies them; minified
   245	    // files carry no quotable structure and cost most of the time.
   246	    // Minified files carry no quotable structure and cost most of the time;
   247	    // manifests are dependency lists every crate shares, and are left to the
   248	    // line pass.
   249	    if (!/^.{2000,}$/m.test(text) && !MANIFEST.test(f)) {
   250	      const { tok } = tokens(text);
   251	      for (let i = 0; i + TOKEN_WINDOW <= tok.length; i++) {
   252	        const h = windowHash(tok, i);
   253	        if (!TRACKED_HASHES.has(h)) continue;
   254	        if (isLicence) { LICENCE_WINDOWS.add(h); continue; }
   255	        let set = TOKEN_HITS.get(h);
   256	        if (!set) TOKEN_HITS.set(h, (set = new Set()));
   257	        set.add(project(c));
   258	      }
   259	    }
   260	    if (c.prose && /\.md$/i.test(f)) {
   261	      const w = words(text);
   262	      for (let i = 0; i + SHINGLE <= w.length; i++) {
   263	        const k = w.slice(i, i + SHINGLE).join(" ");
   264	        let set = SHINGLES.get(k);
   265	        if (!set) SHINGLES.set(k, (set = new Set()));
   266	        set.add(project(c));
   267	      }
   268	    }
   269	  }
   270	  let v = VERSIONS.get(project(c));
   271	  if (!v) VERSIONS.set(project(c), (v = new Set()));
   272	  v.add(c.version);
   273	  return n;
   274	}
   275	
   276	type Run = { file: string; from: number; to: number; kind: "code" | "prose"; projects: string[]; lines: number };
   277	
   278	/**
   279	 * Provenance a matcher cannot infer, written down as a rule. clio builds on
   280	 * rippled's library, so a line or window both hold came from rippled: twice a
   281	 * rippled excerpt in a transcript was attributed to clio because the two share
   282	 * include blocks and licence text. A held set naming both counts for rippled
   283	 * only.
   284	 */
   285	const DERIVED_FROM: Record<string, string> = { "XRPLF/clio": "XRPLF/rippled" };
   286	function provenance(held: Set<string>): Set<string> {
   287	  const out = new Set(held);
   288	  for (const [derived, origin] of Object.entries(DERIVED_FROM)) if (out.has(derived) && out.has(origin)) out.delete(derived);
   289	  return out;
   290	}
   291	const GENERIC_PROJECTS = 3;
   292	const LICENCE_SHARE = 0.8;
   293	
   294	function tracked(): string[] {
   295	  return run(["git", "ls-files"], ROOT).split("\n").filter((f) =>
   296	    f && TEXT.test(f) && !/(^|\/)(bun\.lock|Cargo\.lock|package-lock\.json)$/.test(f)
   297	    && f !== "LICENSE" && f !== "NOTICE" && !f.startsWith("LICENSES/")
   298	    && !f.startsWith("docs/third-party/") && existsSync(join(ROOT, f)));
   299	}
   300	
   301	/**
   302	 * Code runs: consecutive substantive lines, with gaps of up to MAX_GAP, each
   303	 * found in at least one corpus. A run is attributed to every project holding
   304	 * MIN_RUN or more of its lines; it is LICENCE TEXT when most of its lines come
   305	 * from licence files, and SHARED BOILERPLATE when more than GENERIC_PROJECTS
   306	 * projects hold it (Cargo's generated header is in every crate).
   307	 */
   308	function codeRuns(file: string, raw: string[]): { attributed: Run[]; licence: Run[]; generic: Run[] } {
   309	  const isPatch = file.endsWith(".patch");
   310	  const lines = raw.map((l) => norm(stripPrefix(isPatch && /^[ +-]/.test(l) && !/^(\+\+\+|---) /.test(l) ? l.slice(1) : l)));
   311	  const out = { attributed: [] as Run[], licence: [] as Run[], generic: [] as Run[] };
   312	  let run: number[] = [];
   313	  let gap = 0;
   314	  const close = () => {
   315	    if (run.length >= MIN_RUN) {
   316	      // Only SPECIFIC lines — held by GENERIC_PROJECTS projects or fewer —
   317	      // count toward attribution. Counting every line let a few shared
   318	      // attributes in a long quotation push the whole of it into boilerplate,
   319	      // and under-attributing is the unsafe direction for a notice.
   320	      const specific = new Map<string, number>();
   321	      const any = new Set<string>();
   322	      let lic = 0;
   323	      for (const i of run) {
   324	        const held = provenance(LINES.get(lines[i])!);
   325	        for (const p of held) any.add(p);
   326	        if (held.size <= GENERIC_PROJECTS) for (const p of held) specific.set(p, (specific.get(p) ?? 0) + 1);
   327	        if (LICENCE_LINES.has(lines[i])) lic++;
   328	      }
   329	      const projects = [...specific].filter(([, k]) => k >= MIN_RUN).map(([p]) => p).sort();
   330	      const r: Run = { file, from: run[0] + 1, to: run[run.length - 1] + 1, kind: "code", projects: projects.length ? projects : [...any].sort(), lines: run.length };
   331	      if (lic / run.length >= LICENCE_SHARE) out.licence.push(r);
   332	      else if (projects.length) out.attributed.push(r);
   333	      else if (any.size > GENERIC_PROJECTS) out.generic.push(r);
   334	    }
   335	    run = [];
   336	    gap = 0;
   337	  };
   338	  lines.forEach((l, i) => {
   339	    if (!substantive(l)) { if (run.length && ++gap > MAX_GAP) close(); return; }
   340	    if (LINES.has(l)) { run.push(i); gap = 0; } else close();
   341	  });
   342	  close();
   343	  return out;
   344	}
   345	
   346	/** Prose runs: lines carrying a SHINGLE-word run found in a prose corpus. */
   347	function proseRuns(file: string, raw: string[]): Run[] {
   348	  if (!file.endsWith(".md")) return [];
   349	  const out: Run[] = [];
   350	  raw.forEach((_, i) => {
   351	    const w = words(raw.slice(i, i + 4).join(" "));
   352	    const found = new Set<string>();
   353	    for (let k = 0; k + SHINGLE <= w.length; k++) {
   354	      const s = SHINGLES.get(w.slice(k, k + SHINGLE).join(" "));
   355	      if (s) for (const p of s) found.add(p);
   356	    }
   357	    for (const p of found) {
   358	      const prev = out.findLast((r) => r.projects[0] === p);
   359	      if (prev && i + 1 <= prev.to + 1) { prev.to = i + 1; prev.lines++; }
   360	      else out.push({ file, from: i + 1, to: i + 1, kind: "prose", projects: [p], lines: 1 });
   361	    }
   362	  });
   363	  return out;
   364	}
   365	
   366	/**
   367	 * Token runs: positions whose 24-token window is found in a corpus, with gaps
   368	 * of up to MAX_GAP windows, attributed the same way as line runs: to each
   369	 * project holding MIN_TOKEN_RUN or more SPECIFIC windows, held by
   370	 * GENERIC_PROJECTS projects or fewer.
   371	 */
   372	function tokenRuns(file: string, tok: string[], line: number[]): { attributed: Run[]; generic: Run[] } {
   373	  const out = { attributed: [] as Run[], generic: [] as Run[] };
   374	  let run: number[] = [];
   375	  let gap = 0;
   376	  const close = () => {
   377	    if (run.length >= MIN_TOKEN_RUN) {
   378	      const specific = new Map<string, number>();
   379	      const any = new Set<string>();
   380	      for (const i of run) {
   381	        const held = provenance(TOKEN_HITS.get(windowHash(tok, i))!);
   382	        for (const p of held) any.add(p);
   383	        if (held.size <= GENERIC_PROJECTS) for (const p of held) specific.set(p, (specific.get(p) ?? 0) + 1);
   384	      }
   385	      const projects = [...specific].filter(([, k]) => k >= MIN_TOKEN_RUN).map(([p]) => p).sort();
   386	      const last = run[run.length - 1] + TOKEN_WINDOW - 1;
   387	      const r: Run = { file, from: line[run[0]] + 1, to: line[Math.min(last, line.length - 1)] + 1, kind: "code", projects: projects.length ? projects : [...any].sort(), lines: run.length };
   388	      if (projects.length) out.attributed.push(r);
   389	      else if (any.size > GENERIC_PROJECTS) out.generic.push(r);
   390	    }
   391	    run = [];
   392	    gap = 0;
   393	  };
   394	  for (let i = 0; i + TOKEN_WINDOW <= tok.length; i++) {
   395	    const h = windowValid(tok, i) ? windowHash(tok, i) : -1;
   396	    if (h >= 0 && TOKEN_HITS.has(h) && !LICENCE_WINDOWS.has(h)) { run.push(i); gap = 0; }
   397	    else if (run.length && ++gap > MAX_GAP) close();
   398	  }
   399	  close();
   400	  return out;
   401	}
   402	
   403	const all = corpora();
   404	const notCompared = all.filter((c) => !c.dir);
   405	
   406	// The tracked side first, so a corpus can be streamed against its hashes.
   407	const TRACKED = tracked().map((f) => {
   408	  const raw = readFileSync(join(ROOT, f), "utf8").split("\n");
   409	  const isPatch = f.endsWith(".patch");
   410	  const stripped = raw.map((l) => stripPrefix(isPatch && /^[ +-]/.test(l) && !/^(\+\+\+|---) /.test(l) ? l.slice(1) : l));
   411	  const { tok, line } = tokens(stripped.join("\n"));
   412	  for (let i = 0; i + TOKEN_WINDOW <= tok.length; i++) if (windowValid(tok, i)) TRACKED_HASHES.add(windowHash(tok, i));
   413	  return { f, raw, stripped, tok, line };
   414	});
   415	
   416	for (const c of all) {
   417	  if (!c.dir) continue;
   418	  console.error(`${c.name}@${c.version}: ${index(c)} substantive lines`);
   419	}
   420	const LICENCE_OF = new Map(all.filter((c) => c.dir).map((c) => [project(c), c.licence]));
   421	
   422	// A run found by both passes is reported once per pass; the table merges them.
   423	const attributed: Run[] = [], licenceRuns: Run[] = [], genericRuns: Run[] = [];
   424	for (const { f, raw, stripped, tok, line } of TRACKED) {
   425	  const r = codeRuns(f, raw);
   426	  const k = tokenRuns(f, tok, line);
   427	  attributed.push(...r.attributed, ...k.attributed, ...proseRuns(f, stripped));
   428	  licenceRuns.push(...r.licence);
   429	  genericRuns.push(...r.generic, ...k.generic);
   430	}
   431	
   432	/**
   433	 * Hits reviewed by hand and judged not a quotation of the project they name. A
   434	 * matcher finds shared text; it cannot tell which project a shared line came
   435	 * from (`REVIEW-057` R2-5). Each entry is one project, one file and one range,
   436	 * with its reason, and an entry that no longer matches a hit FAILS the check,
   437	 * so a stale allowance cannot outlive what it was written for.
   438	 */
   439	// ⚠ Empty since the DERIVED_FROM rule: its one entry, a rippled include block
   440	// attributed to clio, is now handled by the rule, and a second instance in
   441	// another transcript showed an exception per range would keep growing.
   442	const SET_ASIDE: { project: string; file: string; from: number; to: number; reason: string }[] = [];
   443	const setAside: { entry: (typeof SET_ASIDE)[number]; run: Run }[] = [];
   444	const usedAside = new Set<number>();
   445	for (const r of attributed) {
   446	  r.projects = r.projects.filter((p) => {
   447	    const k = SET_ASIDE.findIndex((e) => e.project === p && e.file === r.file && r.from <= e.to && r.to >= e.from);
   448	    if (k < 0) return true;
   449	    usedAside.add(k);
   450	    setAside.push({ entry: SET_ASIDE[k], run: r });
   451	    return false;
   452	  });
   453	}
   454	const staleAside = SET_ASIDE.filter((_, k) => !usedAside.has(k));
   455	
   456	const byProject = new Map<string, Run[]>();
   457	for (const r of attributed.filter((x) => x.projects.length)) for (const p of r.projects) {
   458	  if (!byProject.has(p)) byProject.set(p, []);
   459	  byProject.get(p)!.push(r);
   460	}
   461	const range = (r: { from: number; to: number }) => (r.from === r.to ? `${r.from}` : `${r.from}–${r.to}`);
   462	/** Overlapping and adjacent ranges merged, because two passes can find one quotation. */
   463	function merged(rs: Run[]): string {
   464	  const sorted = rs.map((r) => ({ from: r.from, to: r.to })).sort((a, b) => a.from - b.from);
   465	  const out: { from: number; to: number }[] = [];
   466	  for (const r of sorted) {
   467	    const last = out[out.length - 1];
   468	    if (last && r.from <= last.to + 1) last.to = Math.max(last.to, r.to); else out.push({ ...r });
   469	  }
   470	  return out.map(range).join(", ");
   471	}
   472	const table = (runs: Run[], withProjects: boolean) => {
   473	  const byFile = new Map<string, Run[]>();
   474	  for (const r of runs) { if (!byFile.has(r.file)) byFile.set(r.file, []); byFile.get(r.file)!.push(r); }
   475	  let s = withProjects ? "| file | lines | shared by |\n|---|---|---|\n" : "| file | kind | lines |\n|---|---|---|\n";
   476	  for (const [f, rs] of byFile) {
   477	    s += withProjects
   478	      ? `| \`${f}\` | ${merged(rs)} | ${[...new Set(rs.flatMap((r) => r.projects))].length} projects |\n`
   479	      : `| \`${f}\` | ${[...new Set(rs.map((r) => r.kind))].join(", ")} | ${merged(rs)} |\n`;
   480	  }
   481	  return s;
   482	};
   483	
   484	let md = `# Third-party inventory
   485	
   486	⚠⚠ **GENERATED. DO NOT HAND-EDIT.** Rebuild with \`bun run tools/inventory-third-party.ts\`.
   487	
   488	Which tracked files reproduce upstream source or specification text. \`NOTICE\` is
   489	written from this file. Method: code is **${MIN_RUN} or more substantive lines in a row**,
   490	with gaps of up to ${MAX_GAP}, each verbatim in a corpus after collapsing whitespace, and
   491	is attributed to every project holding ${MIN_RUN} or more of the run's lines; prose is
   492	**a run of ${SHINGLE} consecutive words**, searched in the specification and documentation
   493	corpora only. A substantive line has ${MIN_LINE} or more characters and a letter. A run
   494	is **licence text** when ${LICENCE_SHARE * 100}% of its lines come from licence files, and
   495	**shared boilerplate** when more than ${GENERIC_PROJECTS} projects hold it; neither is
   496	attributed, and only lines held by ${GENERIC_PROJECTS} projects or fewer count toward
   497	attribution. ⚠ **What this cannot see**: a quotation shorter than those thresholds, a
   498	paraphrase, and a source that is not a corpus below. Sources the records are known to
   499	cite and that are **not** corpora: the Trail of Bits report and the Dore and Cenk papers,
   500	which are PDFs under \`docs/references/\` and not in version control;
   501	\`bitcoin-core/secp256k1\`; and web pages quoted a sentence at a time, such as GitHub's
   502	documentation; and diff-prefixed (\`+\`/\`-\`) quotations inside Markdown, outside a
   503	\`.patch\` file.
   504	
   505	**What is compared.** On the tracked side, every file \`git ls-files\` lists with a text
   506	extension (${TRACKED.length} files), except lockfiles, \`LICENSE\`, \`NOTICE\`, \`LICENSES/\`
   507	and this directory; lines lose a line-number prefix first. On the upstream side, ${all.length}
   508	corpora are configured in ${VERSIONS.size + new Set(notCompared.map((c) => c.name)).size} projects:
   509	**${all.length - notCompared.length} were compared and ${notCompared.length} were unavailable**, listed
   510	at the end. A corpus's files over 2 MB, and minified files, are not tokenised.
   511	
   512	**${byProject.size} projects reproduced.**
   513	
   514	`;
   515	for (const [p, runs] of [...byProject].sort()) {
   516	  md += `## ${p} — ${LICENCE_OF.get(p)}\n\nCompared at ${[...VERSIONS.get(p)!].map((v) => `\`${v}\``).join(", ")}.\n\n${table(runs, false)}\n`;
   517	}
   518	md += `## Reviewed and set aside\n\n${setAside.length ? setAside.map(({ entry, run }) => `- ${entry.project} in \`${entry.file}\` ${range(run)}: ${entry.reason}`).join("\n") + "\n" : "None.\n"}\n`;
   519	md += `## Licence text, not attributed\n\n${licenceRuns.length ? table(licenceRuns, true) : "None.\n"}\n`;
   520	md += `## Shared boilerplate, not attributed\n\n${genericRuns.length ? table(genericRuns, true) : "None.\n"}\n`;
   521	if (notCompared.length) {
   522	  md += `## Not compared\n\n${notCompared.map((c) => `- ${c.name} \`${c.version}\`: ${c.licence}`).join("\n")}\n`;
   523	}
   524	mkdirSync(join(ROOT, "docs/third-party"), { recursive: true });
   525	writeFileSync(OUT, md);
   526	console.log(`wrote ${relative(ROOT, OUT)} — ${byProject.size} projects reproduced, ${licenceRuns.length} licence-text and ${genericRuns.length} boilerplate runs set aside`);
   527	const found = [...byProject.keys()];
   528	
   529	/**
   530	 * The NOTICE check, as a manifest rather than a name search (`REVIEW-057`
   531	 * R2-6, where a NOTICE of six bare names and a deleted licence file passed).
   532	 * For every project found: NOTICE must name it in a bullet; a project with a
   533	 * licence must name a LICENSES/ file in that bullet, and the file must exist
   534	 * and be byte-identical to one of the project's own root licence files; a
   535	 * project with none must be stated as carrying no licence.
   536	 */
   537	function noticeProblems(): string[] {
   538	  const path = join(ROOT, "NOTICE");
   539	  if (!existsSync(path)) return ["NOTICE does not exist"];
   540	  const notice = readFileSync(path, "utf8");
   541	  const problems: string[] = staleAside.map((e) => `set-aside entry for ${e.project} in ${e.file} ${e.from}–${e.to} matches no hit; delete it`);
   542	  for (const p of found) {
   543	    const name = p.replace(/^(crate|npm) /, "");
   544	    const at = notice.indexOf(name);
   545	    if (at < 0) { problems.push(`NOTICE does not name ${name}`); continue; }
   546	    const start = notice.lastIndexOf("\n", at) + 1;
   547	    const next = notice.slice(at).search(/\n\s*(-|\d+\.) |\n\n/);
   548	    const entry = notice.slice(start, next < 0 ? undefined : at + next);
   549	    const lic = LICENCE_OF.get(p) ?? "";
   550	    if (/no licence/i.test(lic)) {
   551	      if (!/no licence/i.test(entry)) problems.push(`NOTICE's entry for ${name} does not say it carries no licence`);
   552	      continue;
   553	    }
   554	    const file = entry.match(/LICENSES\/[\w.-]+/)?.[0];
   555	    if (!file) { problems.push(`NOTICE's entry for ${name} names no LICENSES/ file`); continue; }
   556	    if (!existsSync(join(ROOT, file))) { problems.push(`${file}, named for ${name}, does not exist`); continue; }
   557	    const ours = readFileSync(join(ROOT, file));
   558	    if (!(LICENCE_FILES.get(p) ?? []).some((b) => b.equals(ours))) {
   559	      problems.push(`${file} is not byte-identical to any root licence file of ${name}`);
   560	    }
   561	  }
   562	  return problems;
   563	}
   564	
   565	if (process.argv.includes("--check")) {
   566	  const problems = noticeProblems();
   567	  if (problems.length) {
   568	    for (const x of problems) console.log(`check: ${x}`);
   569	    console.log(`check: FAIL — ${problems.length} problem(s)`);
   570	    process.exit(1);
   571	  }
   572	  console.log(`check: PASS — every one of ${found.length} projects found has a NOTICE entry, and each licensed one a verbatim LICENSES/ file`);
   573	}

    33	The additional experiments found:
    34	
    35	- **R2-1 | HIGH | Git replacement refs hide private ancestry from the running hook | `tools/git-hooks/pre-push:95–105` | Disable replacement objects for ancestry/object inspection and reject grafts, or remove the ancestry exemption.**
    36	
    37	  I installed the real guard in a toy private repository. An ordinary push to a local bare repository was refused. After replacing the private tip with an unrelated orphan commit using `git replace`, the same push succeeded. The receiver contained the **original private commits**, their private root as an ancestor, and the toy private file. The hook inspected Git’s substituted graph; the transmitted history was the original graph.
    38	
    39	- **R2-2 | HIGH | Installation in a shallow clone records a truncation boundary as the private root | `tools/install-push-guard.sh:42–48`; `tools/git-hooks/pre-push:83–105` | Refuse shallow installation and shallow execution; record and validate the actual private root.**
    40	
    41	  The installer accepted a depth-one clone and recorded its tip as the root. After unshallowing, pushing an older private commit to another local bare repository succeeded: that older commit does not descend from the recorded truncation point. The receiver’s private ref was verified.
    42	
    43	- **R2-3 | HIGH | Archive identity is resolved on an unpinned API host | `tools/install-push-guard.sh:37`; `tools/git-hooks/pre-push:114` | Pass `--hostname github.com` in both places.**
    44	
    45	  Both URLs are parsed as GitHub.com destinations, but both `gh api repos/...` calls inherit `GH_HOST`. A real `gh` probe with a synthetic enterprise host attempted that host rather than `api.github.com`.
    46	
    47	  With a host-aware fake API, the hook refused a GitHub.com destination when it returned that destination’s different ID, then approved the same destination when `GH_HOST` selected an enterprise response matching the configured archive ID. Installation under that environment can likewise record the enterprise repository instead of the actual GitHub.com archive. Repository IDs must be bound to their host.
    48	
    49	- **R2-4 | MEDIUM | The gate misses removal of the non-commit-object protection | `tools/git-hooks/pre-push:95–98`; `tools/check-push-guard.sh:144–171,235–245` | Add actual blob/tree/tag-to-blob push cases and corresponding mutants.**
    50	
    51	  My additional mutant changed the failed `^{commit}` conversion branch to `exit 0`. The entire gate still reported **PASS, 26 cases, 11 mutants**. The original hook refused a private blob pushed as a tag; the surviving mutant allowed it, and the local receiver contained the private blob.
    52	
    53	Other coverage: owner/repository and hostname case, userinfo, ports, `.git` and trailing slashes, SSH/scp forms, local paths, `file://`, `ext::` and helper-style URLs were exercised against the parser. Local `--all`, `--mirror` and `--tags` pushes carrying the ordinary private graph were refused. Annotated tags to private commits were covered by the stock gate. Blob/tree refs were conservatively refused by the original hook; deletions and unrelated orphan commits were allowed. Ordinary descendants created after installation remain covered; the shallow test demonstrates a root that becomes visible later and escapes coverage.
    54	
    55	Missing configuration, missing recorded objects, missing `gh`, API failure and visibility changes fail closed in the tested cases. The ancestry status-1 path is the consequential false-negative path.

    43	diagram, which tokenised as punctuation; licence text carried in source-file
    44	headers; and SHA-256's published round constants in `signer/build.rs`. Tokens are
    45	now identifiers and numbers only, a window must be at least half names, and windows
    46	found in any project's licence file never count. **One hit needed a person**: a
    47	rippled include block in a transcript, which clio also carries because it builds on

    51	rippled. The hand list is kept, empty, and an entry that stops matching fails the

/bin/zsh -lc 'nl -ba tools/git-hooks/pre-push
nl -ba tools/install-push-guard.sh
nl -ba tools/check-push-guard.sh' in $USER_TMP/T/review057r3.NMFyrF/repo
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# wconf push guard — the pre-push hook for this repository's PRIVATE clones.
     3	#
     4	# Decision request phase 1, D4 and D8. Installed by `make install-push-guard`,
     5	# which copies this file into the clone's hooks directory and records the
     6	# private archive's GitHub repository id in the clone's git config.
     7	#
     8	# The property: a private clone pushes to the private archive and nowhere else,
     9	# whatever it pushes. "The archive" means the destination resolves, on
    10	# github.com, to the recorded repository id, and GitHub reports it private. The
    11	# id survives a rename, so the check holds before the rename, after it, and
    12	# while the new public repository is still private. Publication happens from
    13	# the separate export, which has no history to protect and no guard.
    14	#
    15	# ⚠ It first tried to decide whether a push CARRIED the private history, and
    16	# allowed anything that did not. `REVIEW-057` round two defeated that three
    17	# ways: a `git replace` ref hid the ancestry from the hook while the original
    18	# history was sent, a shallow install recorded the wrong root, and a non-commit
    19	# object reached a fail-open branch no case covered. A private clone has no
    20	# legitimate destination but the archive, so the judgement is gone.
    21	#
    22	# Measured on git 2.50.1: git passes the destination AFTER `insteadOf`,
    23	# `pushInsteadOf` and `pushurl` are applied, and runs the hook once for every
    24	# push URL of a remote.
    25	#
    26	# ⚠ A guard against mistakes, not against the owner. `git push --no-verify`
    27	# skips it, and it does nothing in a clone it was never installed in.
    28	#
    29	# `make check-push-guard` breaks it every way it knows and fails if any break
    30	# goes unnoticed.
    31	set -u
    32	
    33	# Prints `owner/name` for a GitHub URL, or fails. Hosts are matched exactly, so
    34	# `github.com.example.org` is not GitHub.
    35	parse_github_url() {
    36	  local u=$1 rest hostpart host path
    37	  case "$u" in
    38	    https://* | http://* | ssh://* | git://*)
    39	      rest=${u#*://}
    40	      hostpart=${rest%%/*}
    41	      [ "$hostpart" = "$rest" ] && return 1
    42	      path=${rest#*/}
    43	      ;;
    44	    *:*)
    45	      # scp-like `[user@]host:path`, which git takes only when no `/` comes
    46	      # before the first `:`.
    47	      hostpart=${u%%:*}
    48	      case "$hostpart" in */*) return 1 ;; esac
    49	      path=${u#*:}
    50	      ;;
    51	    *) return 1 ;;
    52	  esac
    53	  hostpart=${hostpart##*@}
    54	  host=$(printf '%s' "${hostpart%%:*}" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')
    55	  case "$host" in
    56	    github.com | www.github.com | ssh.github.com) ;;
    57	    *) return 1 ;;
    58	  esac
    59	  while [ "${path%/}" != "$path" ]; do path=${path%/}; done
    60	  path=${path%.git}
    61	  while [ "${path%/}" != "$path" ]; do path=${path%/}; done
    62	  case "$path" in
    63	    */*/* | /* | */ | '') return 1 ;;
    64	    ?*/?*) ;;
    65	    *) return 1 ;;
    66	  esac
    67	  printf '%s\n' "$path"
    68	}
    69	
    70	if [ "$#" -eq 2 ] && [ "$1" = "--parse-url" ]; then
    71	  parse_github_url "$2"
    72	  exit
    73	fi
    74	
    75	url=${2-}
    76	refuse() {
    77	  printf 'push guard: REFUSED — %s\n' "$1" >&2
    78	  printf '  destination: %s\n' "$url" >&2
    79	  printf '  A private clone pushes only to the private archive. Publish from the\n' >&2
    80	  printf '  export instead: decision request phase 1, item 5, step 5.\n' >&2
    81	  exit 1
    82	}
    83	
    84	archive_id=$(git config --get wconf.pushguard.archiveid || true)
    85	[ -n "$archive_id" ] || refuse "the guard is not configured in this clone; run make install-push-guard"
    86	
    87	target=$(parse_github_url "$url") \
    88	  || refuse "the destination is not a repository on github.com"
    89	command -v gh >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    90	  || refuse "gh is not on PATH, so the destination cannot be checked"
    91	# `--hostname` pins the lookup to github.com. Without it gh follows GH_HOST, and
    92	# a destination parsed as github.com would be checked against another host's
    93	# repository ids (`REVIEW-057` R2-3).
    94	info=$(gh api --hostname github.com "repos/$target" --jq '"\(.id) \(.private)"' 2>/dev/null) \
    95	  || refuse "repos/$target could not be read from github.com"
    96	id=${info%% *}
    97	private=${info#* }
    98	[ "$id" = "$archive_id" ] \
    99	  || refuse "$target (repository id $id) is not the archive (id $archive_id)"
   100	[ "$private" = "true" ] \
   101	  || refuse "$target is the archive, but GitHub reports that it is not private"
   102	exit 0
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Installs the push guard, tools/git-hooks/pre-push, in this clone.
     3	#
     4	# Run it in every PRIVATE clone (decision request phase 1, D4 and item 5,
     5	# step 8). It records the archive as the GitHub repository behind the clone's
     6	# own push URL — so run it while `origin` is the private archive, under either
     7	# name — and refuses if GitHub does not report that repository private.
     8	#
     9	# The hook is COPIED, not linked. A link into the tracked file would vanish on
    10	# checking out any commit that predates it, and git treats a missing hook as
    11	# no hook.
    12	#
    13	# Usage: tools/install-push-guard.sh [remote]   (default: origin)
    14	set -euo pipefail
    15	
    16	fail() { printf 'install-push-guard: REFUSED — %s\n' "$1" >&2; exit 1; }
    17	
    18	top=$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)
    19	src="$top/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
    20	[ -f "$src" ] || fail "$src is missing"
    21	
    22	if hooks_path=$(git config --get core.hooksPath); then
    23	  fail "core.hooksPath is set ($hooks_path), so a hook copied into the hooks directory would not run"
    24	fi
    25	hooks_dir=$(git rev-parse --git-path hooks)
    26	case "$hooks_dir" in /*) ;; *) hooks_dir="$top/$hooks_dir" ;; esac
    27	dest="$hooks_dir/pre-push"
    28	if [ -e "$dest" ] && ! grep -q '^# wconf push guard' "$dest"; then
    29	  fail "$dest exists and is not this guard; move it aside first"
    30	fi
    31	
    32	remote=${1:-origin}
    33	push_url=$(git remote get-url --push "$remote") || fail "no remote named $remote"
    34	target=$(bash "$src" --parse-url "$push_url") \
    35	  || fail "$remote's push URL is not a GitHub repository: $push_url"
    36	command -v gh >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "gh is not on PATH"
    37	info=$(gh api --hostname github.com "repos/$target" --jq '"\(.id) \(.private) \(.full_name)"') \
    38	  || fail "could not read repos/$target from GitHub"
    39	read -r archive_id private full_name <<<"$info"
    40	[ "$private" = "true" ] || fail "$full_name is not private, so it cannot be the archive"
    41	
    42	git config wconf.pushguard.archiveid "$archive_id"
    43	git config wconf.pushguard.archivename "$full_name"
    44	# Earlier versions recorded the history's roots; the hook no longer uses them.
    45	git config --unset wconf.pushguard.roots 2>/dev/null || true
    46	mkdir -p "$hooks_dir"
    47	cp "$src" "$dest"
    48	chmod 0755 "$dest"
    49	
    50	printf 'push guard installed: %s\n' "$dest"
    51	printf '  archive: %s (repository id %s, private)\n' "$full_name" "$archive_id"
    52	printf '  Every push from this clone may go only to that repository id. The guard\n'
    53	printf '  does not run on git push --no-verify, in a clone it was never installed\n'
    54	printf '  in, or if core.hooksPath is set later.\n'
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Self-test for the push guard, tools/git-hooks/pre-push.
     3	#
     4	# Every case runs against the real hook, installed by the real installer into a
     5	# throwaway repository, with a fake `gh` on PATH so nothing touches GitHub.
     6	# Then every case runs again against MUTANTS of the hook, each of which removes
     7	# one protection. A mutant that the cases do not catch fails this check, and so
     8	# does a mutation that does not apply: a case list that a broken hook still
     9	# passes proves nothing. Mutants are evaluated only when the real hook passes
    10	# every case, because otherwise each one would look caught.
    11	#
    12	# The fake gh answers from github.com's table only when it is called with
    13	# `--hostname github.com`. Called without it, it answers from another host's
    14	# table, where the public repository has the archive's id — so a hook that lets
    15	# GH_HOST choose the host is caught (`REVIEW-057` R2-3).
    16	#
    17	# Needs bash, git and perl. No network.
    18	set -u
    19	
    20	top=$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)
    21	HOOK_SRC="$top/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
    22	INSTALL_SRC="$top/tools/install-push-guard.sh"
    23	work=$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/push-guard.XXXXXX")
    24	trap 'rm -rf "$work"' EXIT
    25	
    26	G() {
    27	  git -c user.name=guard -c user.email=guard@example.invalid \
    28	      -c commit.gpgsign=false -c tag.gpgsign=false -c init.defaultBranch=main "$@"
    29	}
    30	ZERO=0000000000000000000000000000000000000000
    31	FAILED=0
    32	fail() { printf '  ✗ %s\n' "$1"; FAILED=$((FAILED + 1)); }
    33	pass() { printf '  ✓ %s\n' "$1"; }
    34	
    35	if hp=$(git config --global --get core.hooksPath); then
    36	  printf 'check-push-guard: FAIL — core.hooksPath is set globally (%s), so the guard cannot run on this machine\n' "$hp"
    37	  exit 1
    38	fi
    39	
    40	# --- a fake gh: `gh api [--hostname H] repos/<owner>/<name> --jq <expr>`
    41	mkdir -p "$work/bin" "$work/minbin"
    42	cat >"$work/bin/gh" <<'EOF'
    43	#!/usr/bin/env bash
    44	[ "${1-}" = api ] || exit 2
    45	shift
    46	host= repo= expr=
    47	while [ "$#" -gt 0 ]; do
    48	  case "$1" in
    49	    --hostname) host=$2; shift 2 ;;
    50	    --jq) expr=$2; shift 2 ;;
    51	    repos/*) repo=${1#repos/}; shift ;;
    52	    *) shift ;;
    53	  esac
    54	done
    55	table=$FAKE_GH_TABLE
    56	[ "$host" = github.com ] || table=$FAKE_GH_OTHER_HOST
    57	want=$(printf '%s' "$repo" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')
    58	while read -r name id private; do
    59	  if [ "$(printf '%s' "$name" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')" = "$want" ]; then
    60	    case "$expr" in
    61	      *full_name*) printf '%s %s %s\n' "$id" "$private" "$name" ;;
    62	      *) printf '%s %s\n' "$id" "$private" ;;
    63	    esac
    64	    exit 0
    65	  fi
    66	done <"$table"
    67	echo 'HTTP 404: Not Found' >&2
    68	exit 1
    69	EOF
    70	chmod +x "$work/bin/gh"
    71	for t in bash git tr; do ln -s "$(command -v "$t")" "$work/minbin/$t"; done
    72	PATH_GH="$work/bin:$PATH"
    73	PATH_NO_GH="$work/minbin"
    74	
    75	TABLE="$work/table"
    76	printf 'arch/ive 100 true\npub/lic 200 true\nflip/ped 300 false\n' >"$TABLE"
    77	TABLE_FLIPPED="$work/table-flipped"
    78	printf 'arch/ive 100 false\npub/lic 200 true\n' >"$TABLE_FLIPPED"
    79	# Another host, where the public repository's name resolves to the archive's id.
    80	OTHER="$work/table-other-host"
    81	printf 'arch/ive 999 true\npub/lic 100 true\n' >"$OTHER"
    82	export FAKE_GH_OTHER_HOST="$OTHER"
    83	
    84	# --- the "private" clone, with the guard installed
    85	P="$work/private"
    86	G init -q "$P"
    87	G -C "$P" commit -q --allow-empty -m root
    88	G -C "$P" commit -q --allow-empty -m second
    89	PRIV=$(G -C "$P" rev-parse HEAD)
    90	mkdir -p "$P/tools/git-hooks"
    91	cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$P/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
    92	cp "$INSTALL_SRC" "$P/tools/install-push-guard.sh"
    93	G -C "$P" remote add origin https://github.com/arch/ive.git
    94	
    95	echo "The installer:"
    96	if out=$(cd "$P" && PATH="$PATH_GH" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$TABLE" GH_HOST=ghe.example.invalid bash tools/install-push-guard.sh 2>&1); then
    97	  pass "installs against a private archive"
    98	else
    99	  fail "installs against a private archive: $out"
   100	fi
   101	[ "$(G -C "$P" config --get wconf.pushguard.archiveid)" = 100 ] \
   102	  && pass "records the archive's id from github.com, with GH_HOST set elsewhere" \
   103	  || fail "records the archive's id from github.com, with GH_HOST set elsewhere"
   104	cmp -s "$HOOK_SRC" "$P/.git/hooks/pre-push" \
   105	  && pass "copies the hook byte for byte" || fail "copies the hook byte for byte"
   106	
   107	installer_refuses() { # <label> <needle> <setup...>
   108	  local label=$1 needle=$2 d="$work/inst.$RANDOM"
   109	  shift 2
   110	  G init -q "$d" && G -C "$d" commit -q --allow-empty -m root
   111	  mkdir -p "$d/tools/git-hooks"
   112	  cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$d/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
   113	  cp "$INSTALL_SRC" "$d/tools/install-push-guard.sh"
   114	  (cd "$d" && "$@")
   115	  local o rc
   116	  o=$(cd "$d" && PATH="$PATH_GH" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$TABLE" bash tools/install-push-guard.sh 2>&1)
   117	  rc=$?
   118	  if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && printf '%s' "$o" | grep -qF "$needle"; then pass "$label"; else fail "$label (exit $rc): $o"; fi
   119	}
   120	installer_refuses "refuses an origin that is not private" "is not private" \
   121	  git remote add origin https://github.com/flip/ped.git
   122	installer_refuses "refuses when core.hooksPath is set" "core.hooksPath is set" \
   123	  sh -c 'git remote add origin https://github.com/arch/ive.git && git config core.hooksPath .hooks'
   124	installer_refuses "refuses to overwrite a foreign pre-push hook" "is not this guard" \
   125	  sh -c 'git remote add origin https://github.com/arch/ive.git && printf "#!/bin/sh\nexit 0\n" > .git/hooks/pre-push'
   126	installer_refuses "refuses an origin that is not on GitHub" "not a GitHub repository" \
   127	  git remote add origin "$work/somewhere.git"
   128	
   129	# The installer's own lookup must be pinned too: a copy without --hostname must
   130	# record the other host's id, or the check above could not have failed.
   131	d="$work/inst-unpinned"
   132	G init -q "$d" && G -C "$d" commit -q --allow-empty -m root
   133	mkdir -p "$d/tools/git-hooks"
   134	cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$d/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
   135	sed 's/gh api --hostname github.com /gh api /' "$INSTALL_SRC" >"$d/tools/install-push-guard.sh"
   136	G -C "$d" remote add origin https://github.com/arch/ive.git
   137	(cd "$d" && PATH="$PATH_GH" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$TABLE" bash tools/install-push-guard.sh >/dev/null 2>&1)
   138	if cmp -s "$INSTALL_SRC" "$d/tools/install-push-guard.sh"; then
   139	  fail "control: the installer mutation did not apply"
   140	elif [ "$(G -C "$d" config --get wconf.pushguard.archiveid)" = 999 ]; then
   141	  pass "control: an installer without --hostname records the wrong id, so the check above can fail"
   142	else
   143	  fail "control: an installer without --hostname still recorded the right id"
   144	fi
   145	
   146	# A clone with the hook but no configuration.
   147	U="$work/unconfigured"
   148	G init -q "$U" && G -C "$U" commit -q --allow-empty -m root
   149	cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$U/.git/hooks/pre-push"
   150	
   151	G -C "$P" checkout -q --orphan clean
   152	G -C "$P" commit -q --allow-empty -m orphan
   153	ORPH=$(G -C "$P" rev-parse HEAD)
   154	G -C "$P" checkout -q main
   155	
   156	ref() { printf 'refs/heads/main %s refs/heads/main %s\n' "$1" "$ZERO"; }
   157	
   158	# One case: <want 0|1> <needle or -> <url> <stdin> <repo> <PATH> <table> [GH_HOST]
   159	case_result() {
   160	  local want=$1 needle=$2 url=$3 input=$4 repo=$5 path=$6 table=$7 ghhost=${8-}
   161	  local out rc
   162	  out=$(cd "$repo" && printf '%s' "$input" | env ${ghhost:+GH_HOST=$ghhost} PATH="$path" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$table" "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push" origin "$url" 2>&1)
   163	  rc=$?
   164	  if [ "$want" = 0 ]; then
   165	    [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]
   166	  else
   167	    [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && { [ "$needle" = - ] || printf '%s' "$out" | grep -qF "$needle"; }
   168	  fi
   169	}
   170	
   171	NOT_ARCHIVE="is not the archive"
   172	NOT_GITHUB="is not a repository on github.com"
   173	CASES=(
   174	  "A1|0|-|https://github.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   175	  "A2|0|-|git@github.com:arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   176	  "A3|0|-|https://GITHUB.COM/Arch/Ive.git/|PRIV"
   177	  "A4|0|-|ssh://ssh.github.com:443/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   178	  "A5|0|-|https://github.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV|GHHOST"
   179	  "R1|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV"
   180	  "R2|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|git@github.com:pub/lic.git|PRIV"
   181	  "R3|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|ssh://git@github.com/pub/lic|PRIV"
   182	  "R4|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/PUB/LIC.git/|PRIV"
   183	  "R5|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://git@github.com/pub/lic|PRIV"
   184	  "R6|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic//|PRIV"
   185	  "R7|1|$NOT_GITHUB|$work/public.git|PRIV"
   186	  "R8|1|$NOT_GITHUB|file://$work/public.git|PRIV"
   187	  "R9|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://github.com.example.org/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   188	  "R10|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://example.org/github.com/arch/ive|PRIV"
   189	  "R11|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://gitlab.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   190	  "R12|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://github.com/arch/ive/extra|PRIV"
   191	  "R13|1|could not be read|https://github.com/no/such|PRIV"
   192	  "R14|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|DELETE"
   193	  "R15|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|ORPH"
   194	  "R16|1|gh is not on PATH|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV|NOGH"
   195	  "R17|1|is not private|https://github.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV|FLIPPED"
   196	  "R18|1|not configured|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV|UNCONF"
   197	  "R19|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV|GHHOST"
   198	  "R20|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|EMPTY"
   199	)
   200	CASE_COUNT=${#CASES[@]}
   201	
   202	# Runs every case; prints the names that fail, one per line.
   203	run_cases() {
   204	  local c name want needle url what mode input repo path table ghhost
   205	  for c in "${CASES[@]}"; do
   206	    IFS='|' read -r name want needle url what mode <<<"$c"
   207	    repo=$P path=$PATH_GH table=$TABLE ghhost=
   208	    case "$what" in
   209	      PRIV) input=$(ref "$PRIV") ;;
   210	      ORPH) input=$(ref "$ORPH") ;;
   211	      DELETE) input=$(printf '(delete) %s refs/heads/old %s\n' "$ZERO" "$PRIV") ;;
   212	      EMPTY) input= ;;
   213	    esac
   214	    case "${mode-}" in
   215	      NOGH) path=$PATH_NO_GH ;;
   216	      FLIPPED) table=$TABLE_FLIPPED ;;
   217	      UNCONF) repo=$U ;;
   218	      GHHOST) ghhost=ghe.example.invalid ;;
   219	    esac
   220	    case_result "$want" "$needle" "$url" "$input" "$repo" "$path" "$table" "$ghhost" || echo "$name"
   221	  done
   222	}
   223	
   224	echo
   225	echo "The hook, ${CASE_COUNT} cases:"
   226	failed_cases=$(run_cases)
   227	if [ -z "$failed_cases" ]; then
   228	  pass "all ${CASE_COUNT} cases"
   229	else
   230	  for n in $failed_cases; do fail "case $n"; done
   231	fi
   232	
   233	echo
   234	echo "Through git itself:"
   235	G init -q --bare "$work/public.git"
   236	pushes() { # <label> <git push args...>: the push must fail and deliver nothing
   237	  local label=$1
   238	  shift
   239	  if (cd "$P" && PATH="$PATH_GH" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$TABLE" G push -q "$@" 2>/dev/null); then
   240	    fail "$label"
   241	  elif [ -n "$(G -C "$work/public.git" for-each-ref)" ]; then
   242	    fail "$label (but refs arrived)"
   243	  else
   244	    pass "$label"
   245	  fi
   246	}
   247	pushes "a real push of the private history is refused, and nothing arrives" "$work/public.git" main
   248	pushes "a commit without the private history is refused too" "$work/public.git" clean
   249	pushes "a dry run is refused, so the guard can be proved against a real repository safely" --dry-run "$work/public.git" main
   250	# REVIEW-057 R2-1: a replacement ref hid the private ancestry from the old hook
   251	# while git sent the original history.
   252	G -C "$P" replace "$PRIV" "$ORPH"
   253	pushes "a push behind a git replace ref is refused, and nothing arrives" "$work/public.git" main
   254	G -C "$P" replace -d "$PRIV" >/dev/null
   255	
   256	# --- mutants: each removes one protection, and the named case must catch it
   257	echo
   258	echo "Mutants — each must be caught by the case named:"
   259	# Four parallel arrays, because several patterns contain `|`.
   260	M_MUST=() M_LABEL=() M_FROM=() M_TO=()
   261	mutant() { M_MUST+=("$1"); M_LABEL+=("$2"); M_FROM+=("$3"); M_TO+=("$4"); }
   262	mutant R1 "repository id not compared" '[ "$id" = "$archive_id" ]' 'true'
   263	mutant R7 "a destination off github.com allowed" '|| refuse "the destination is not a repository on github.com"' '|| exit 0'
   264	mutant R9 "host matched loosely" 'github.com | www.github.com | ssh.github.com) ;;' '*github.com*) ;;'
   265	mutant R12 "extra path segments accepted" "*/*/* | /* | */ | '') return 1 ;;" "/* | */ | '') return 1 ;;"
   266	mutant R13 "fail-open when GitHub cannot be read" '|| refuse "repos/$target could not be read from github.com"' '|| exit 0'
   267	mutant R16 "a missing gh ignored" '|| refuse "gh is not on PATH, so the destination cannot be checked"' '|| true'
   268	mutant R17 "archive's visibility not checked" '[ "$private" = "true" ]' 'true'
   269	mutant R18 "unconfigured clone allowed" '|| refuse "the guard is not configured in this clone; run make install-push-guard"' '|| exit 0'
   270	mutant R19 "lookup not pinned to github.com" 'gh api --hostname github.com "repos/$target"' 'gh api "repos/$target"'
   271	MUTANT_COUNT=${#M_MUST[@]}
   272	if [ -n "$failed_cases" ]; then
   273	  fail "no mutant evaluated: the real hook fails cases, so a mutant's failures would prove nothing"
   274	  M_MUST=()
   275	fi
   276	for i in "${!M_MUST[@]}"; do
   277	  must=${M_MUST[$i]} label=${M_LABEL[$i]}
   278	  for repo in "$P" "$U"; do
   279	    cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push"
   280	    FROM=${M_FROM[$i]} TO=${M_TO[$i]} perl -0pi -e 's/\Q$ENV{FROM}\E/$ENV{TO}/' "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push"
   281	  done
   282	  if cmp -s "$HOOK_SRC" "$P/.git/hooks/pre-push"; then
   283	    fail "mutant '$label' did not apply — the pattern no longer matches the hook"
   284	    continue
   285	  fi
   286	  caught=$(run_cases)
   287	  if printf '%s\n' "$caught" | grep -qx "$must"; then
   288	    pass "'$label' is caught by $must"
   289	  else
   290	    fail "'$label' is NOT caught by $must (cases failing: $(printf '%s' "$caught" | tr '\n' ' '))"
   291	  fi
   292	done
   293	for repo in "$P" "$U"; do cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push"; done
   294	
   295	echo
   296	echo "What this does not check:"
   297	echo "  1. git push --no-verify, which skips every hook; a clone the guard was never"
   298	echo "     installed in; and core.hooksPath set after the install."
   299	echo "  2. Real GitHub. gh is faked here. make install-push-guard reads the real"
   300	echo "     archive, and a git push --dry-run to a real public repository proves the"
   301	echo "     refusal without sending anything."
   302	echo
   303	if [ "$FAILED" -eq 0 ]; then
   304	  printf 'check-push-guard: verdict=PASS cases=%d mutants=%d exit=0\n' "$CASE_COUNT" "$MUTANT_COUNT"
   305	  exit 0
   306	fi
   307	printf 'check-push-guard: verdict=FAIL failures=%d exit=1\n' "$FAILED"
   308	exit 1

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# wconf push guard — the pre-push hook for this repository's PRIVATE clones.
     3	#
     4	# Decision request phase 1, D4 and D8. Installed by `make install-push-guard`,
     5	# which copies this file into the clone's hooks directory and records the
     6	# private archive's GitHub repository id in the clone's git config.
     7	#
     8	# The property: a private clone pushes to the private archive and nowhere else,
     9	# whatever it pushes. "The archive" means the destination resolves, on
    10	# github.com, to the recorded repository id, and GitHub reports it private. The
    11	# id survives a rename, so the check holds before the rename, after it, and
    12	# while the new public repository is still private. Publication happens from
    13	# the separate export, which has no history to protect and no guard.
    14	#
    15	# ⚠ It first tried to decide whether a push CARRIED the private history, and
    16	# allowed anything that did not. `REVIEW-057` round two defeated that three
    17	# ways: a `git replace` ref hid the ancestry from the hook while the original
    18	# history was sent, a shallow install recorded the wrong root, and a non-commit
    19	# object reached a fail-open branch no case covered. A private clone has no
    20	# legitimate destination but the archive, so the judgement is gone.
    21	#
    22	# Measured on git 2.50.1: git passes the destination AFTER `insteadOf`,
    23	# `pushInsteadOf` and `pushurl` are applied, and runs the hook once for every
    24	# push URL of a remote.
    25	#
    26	# ⚠ A guard against mistakes, not against the owner. `git push --no-verify`
    27	# skips it, and it does nothing in a clone it was never installed in.
    28	#
    29	# `make check-push-guard` breaks it every way it knows and fails if any break
    30	# goes unnoticed.
    31	set -u
    32	
    33	# Prints `owner/name` for a GitHub URL, or fails. Hosts are matched exactly, so
    34	# `github.com.example.org` is not GitHub.
    35	parse_github_url() {
    36	  local u=$1 rest hostpart host path
    37	  case "$u" in
    38	    https://* | http://* | ssh://* | git://*)
    39	      rest=${u#*://}
    40	      hostpart=${rest%%/*}
    41	      [ "$hostpart" = "$rest" ] && return 1
    42	      path=${rest#*/}
    43	      ;;
    44	    *:*)
    45	      # scp-like `[user@]host:path`, which git takes only when no `/` comes
    46	      # before the first `:`.
    47	      hostpart=${u%%:*}
    48	      case "$hostpart" in */*) return 1 ;; esac
    49	      path=${u#*:}
    50	      ;;
    51	    *) return 1 ;;
    52	  esac
    53	  hostpart=${hostpart##*@}
    54	  host=$(printf '%s' "${hostpart%%:*}" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')
    55	  case "$host" in
    56	    github.com | www.github.com | ssh.github.com) ;;
    57	    *) return 1 ;;
    58	  esac
    59	  while [ "${path%/}" != "$path" ]; do path=${path%/}; done
    60	  path=${path%.git}
    61	  while [ "${path%/}" != "$path" ]; do path=${path%/}; done
    62	  case "$path" in
    63	    */*/* | /* | */ | '') return 1 ;;
    64	    ?*/?*) ;;
    65	    *) return 1 ;;
    66	  esac
    67	  printf '%s\n' "$path"
    68	}
    69	
    70	if [ "$#" -eq 2 ] && [ "$1" = "--parse-url" ]; then
    71	  parse_github_url "$2"
    72	  exit
    73	fi
    74	
    75	url=${2-}
    76	refuse() {
    77	  printf 'push guard: REFUSED — %s\n' "$1" >&2
    78	  printf '  destination: %s\n' "$url" >&2
    79	  printf '  A private clone pushes only to the private archive. Publish from the\n' >&2
    80	  printf '  export instead: decision request phase 1, item 5, step 5.\n' >&2
    81	  exit 1
    82	}
    83	
    84	archive_id=$(git config --get wconf.pushguard.archiveid || true)
    85	[ -n "$archive_id" ] || refuse "the guard is not configured in this clone; run make install-push-guard"
    86	
    87	target=$(parse_github_url "$url") \
    88	  || refuse "the destination is not a repository on github.com"
    89	command -v gh >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    90	  || refuse "gh is not on PATH, so the destination cannot be checked"
    91	# `--hostname` pins the lookup to github.com. Without it gh follows GH_HOST, and
    92	# a destination parsed as github.com would be checked against another host's
    93	# repository ids (`REVIEW-057` R2-3).
    94	info=$(gh api --hostname github.com "repos/$target" --jq '"\(.id) \(.private)"' 2>/dev/null) \
    95	  || refuse "repos/$target could not be read from github.com"
    96	id=${info%% *}
    97	private=${info#* }
    98	[ "$id" = "$archive_id" ] \
    99	  || refuse "$target (repository id $id) is not the archive (id $archive_id)"
   100	[ "$private" = "true" ] \
   101	  || refuse "$target is the archive, but GitHub reports that it is not private"
   102	exit 0
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Installs the push guard, tools/git-hooks/pre-push, in this clone.
     3	#
     4	# Run it in every PRIVATE clone (decision request phase 1, D4 and item 5,
     5	# step 8). It records the archive as the GitHub repository behind the clone's
     6	# own push URL — so run it while `origin` is the private archive, under either
     7	# name — and refuses if GitHub does not report that repository private.
     8	#
     9	# The hook is COPIED, not linked. A link into the tracked file would vanish on
    10	# checking out any commit that predates it, and git treats a missing hook as
    11	# no hook.
    12	#
    13	# Usage: tools/install-push-guard.sh [remote]   (default: origin)
    14	set -euo pipefail
    15	
    16	fail() { printf 'install-push-guard: REFUSED — %s\n' "$1" >&2; exit 1; }
    17	
    18	top=$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)
    19	src="$top/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
    20	[ -f "$src" ] || fail "$src is missing"
    21	
    22	if hooks_path=$(git config --get core.hooksPath); then
    23	  fail "core.hooksPath is set ($hooks_path), so a hook copied into the hooks directory would not run"
    24	fi
    25	hooks_dir=$(git rev-parse --git-path hooks)
    26	case "$hooks_dir" in /*) ;; *) hooks_dir="$top/$hooks_dir" ;; esac
    27	dest="$hooks_dir/pre-push"
    28	if [ -e "$dest" ] && ! grep -q '^# wconf push guard' "$dest"; then
    29	  fail "$dest exists and is not this guard; move it aside first"
    30	fi
    31	
    32	remote=${1:-origin}
    33	push_url=$(git remote get-url --push "$remote") || fail "no remote named $remote"
    34	target=$(bash "$src" --parse-url "$push_url") \
    35	  || fail "$remote's push URL is not a GitHub repository: $push_url"
    36	command -v gh >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "gh is not on PATH"
    37	info=$(gh api --hostname github.com "repos/$target" --jq '"\(.id) \(.private) \(.full_name)"') \
    38	  || fail "could not read repos/$target from GitHub"
    39	read -r archive_id private full_name <<<"$info"
    40	[ "$private" = "true" ] || fail "$full_name is not private, so it cannot be the archive"
    41	
    42	git config wconf.pushguard.archiveid "$archive_id"
    43	git config wconf.pushguard.archivename "$full_name"
    44	# Earlier versions recorded the history's roots; the hook no longer uses them.
    45	git config --unset wconf.pushguard.roots 2>/dev/null || true
    46	mkdir -p "$hooks_dir"
    47	cp "$src" "$dest"
    48	chmod 0755 "$dest"
    49	
    50	printf 'push guard installed: %s\n' "$dest"
    51	printf '  archive: %s (repository id %s, private)\n' "$full_name" "$archive_id"
    52	printf '  Every push from this clone may go only to that repository id. The guard\n'
    53	printf '  does not run on git push --no-verify, in a clone it was never installed\n'
    54	printf '  in, or if core.hooksPath is set later.\n'

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Self-test for the push guard, tools/git-hooks/pre-push.
     3	#
     4	# Every case runs against the real hook, installed by the real installer into a
     5	# throwaway repository, with a fake `gh` on PATH so nothing touches GitHub.
     6	# Then every case runs again against MUTANTS of the hook, each of which removes
     7	# one protection. A mutant that the cases do not catch fails this check, and so
     8	# does a mutation that does not apply: a case list that a broken hook still
     9	# passes proves nothing. Mutants are evaluated only when the real hook passes
    10	# every case, because otherwise each one would look caught.
    11	#
    12	# The fake gh answers from github.com's table only when it is called with
    13	# `--hostname github.com`. Called without it, it answers from another host's
    14	# table, where the public repository has the archive's id — so a hook that lets
    15	# GH_HOST choose the host is caught (`REVIEW-057` R2-3).
    16	#
    17	# Needs bash, git and perl. No network.
    18	set -u
    19	
    20	top=$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)
    21	HOOK_SRC="$top/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
    22	INSTALL_SRC="$top/tools/install-push-guard.sh"
    23	work=$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/push-guard.XXXXXX")
    24	trap 'rm -rf "$work"' EXIT
    25	
    26	G() {
    27	  git -c user.name=guard -c user.email=guard@example.invalid \
    28	      -c commit.gpgsign=false -c tag.gpgsign=false -c init.defaultBranch=main "$@"
    29	}
    30	ZERO=0000000000000000000000000000000000000000
    31	FAILED=0
    32	fail() { printf '  ✗ %s\n' "$1"; FAILED=$((FAILED + 1)); }
    33	pass() { printf '  ✓ %s\n' "$1"; }
    34	
    35	if hp=$(git config --global --get core.hooksPath); then
    36	  printf 'check-push-guard: FAIL — core.hooksPath is set globally (%s), so the guard cannot run on this machine\n' "$hp"
    37	  exit 1
    38	fi
    39	
    40	# --- a fake gh: `gh api [--hostname H] repos/<owner>/<name> --jq <expr>`
    41	mkdir -p "$work/bin" "$work/minbin"
    42	cat >"$work/bin/gh" <<'EOF'
    43	#!/usr/bin/env bash
    44	[ "${1-}" = api ] || exit 2
    45	shift
    46	host= repo= expr=
    47	while [ "$#" -gt 0 ]; do
    48	  case "$1" in
    49	    --hostname) host=$2; shift 2 ;;
    50	    --jq) expr=$2; shift 2 ;;
    51	    repos/*) repo=${1#repos/}; shift ;;
    52	    *) shift ;;
    53	  esac
    54	done
    55	table=$FAKE_GH_TABLE
    56	[ "$host" = github.com ] || table=$FAKE_GH_OTHER_HOST
    57	want=$(printf '%s' "$repo" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')
    58	while read -r name id private; do
    59	  if [ "$(printf '%s' "$name" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')" = "$want" ]; then
    60	    case "$expr" in
    61	      *full_name*) printf '%s %s %s\n' "$id" "$private" "$name" ;;
    62	      *) printf '%s %s\n' "$id" "$private" ;;
    63	    esac
    64	    exit 0
    65	  fi
    66	done <"$table"
    67	echo 'HTTP 404: Not Found' >&2
    68	exit 1
    69	EOF
    70	chmod +x "$work/bin/gh"
    71	for t in bash git tr; do ln -s "$(command -v "$t")" "$work/minbin/$t"; done
    72	PATH_GH="$work/bin:$PATH"
    73	PATH_NO_GH="$work/minbin"
    74	
    75	TABLE="$work/table"
    76	printf 'arch/ive 100 true\npub/lic 200 true\nflip/ped 300 false\n' >"$TABLE"
    77	TABLE_FLIPPED="$work/table-flipped"
    78	printf 'arch/ive 100 false\npub/lic 200 true\n' >"$TABLE_FLIPPED"
    79	# Another host, where the public repository's name resolves to the archive's id.
    80	OTHER="$work/table-other-host"
    81	printf 'arch/ive 999 true\npub/lic 100 true\n' >"$OTHER"
    82	export FAKE_GH_OTHER_HOST="$OTHER"
    83	
    84	# --- the "private" clone, with the guard installed
    85	P="$work/private"
    86	G init -q "$P"
    87	G -C "$P" commit -q --allow-empty -m root
    88	G -C "$P" commit -q --allow-empty -m second
    89	PRIV=$(G -C "$P" rev-parse HEAD)
    90	mkdir -p "$P/tools/git-hooks"
    91	cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$P/tools/git-hooks/pre-push"
    92	cp "$INSTALL_SRC" "$P/tools/install-push-guard.sh"
    93	G -C "$P" remote add origin https://github.com/arch/ive.git
    94	
    95	echo "The installer:"
    96	if out=$(cd "$P" && PATH="$PATH_GH" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$TABLE" GH_HOST=ghe.example.invalid bash tools/install-push-guard.sh 2>&1); then
    97	  pass "installs against a private archive"
    98	else
    99	  fail "installs against a private archive: $out"
   100	fi
   101	[ "$(G -C "$P" config --get wconf.pushguard.archiveid)" = 100 ] \
   102	  && pass "records the archive's id from github.com, with GH_HOST set elsewhere" \
   103	  || fail "records the archive's id from github.com, with GH_HOST set elsewhere"
   104	cmp -s "$HOOK_SRC" "$P/.git/hooks/pre-push" \
   105	  && pass "copies the hook byte for byte" || fail "copies the hook byte for byte"
   106	
   107	installer_refuses() { # <label> <needle> <setup...>
   108	  local label=$1 needle=$2 d="$work/inst.$RANDOM"
   109	  shift 2
   110	  G init -q "$d" && G -C "$d" commit -q --allow-empty -m root
   111	  mkdir -p "$d/tools/git-hooks"

   115	  local o rc
   116	  o=$(cd "$d" && PATH="$PATH_GH" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$TABLE" bash tools/install-push-guard.sh 2>&1)
   117	  rc=$?
   118	  if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && printf '%s' "$o" | grep -qF "$needle"; then pass "$label"; else fail "$label (exit $rc): $o"; fi
   119	}
   120	installer_refuses "refuses an origin that is not private" "is not private" \
   121	  git remote add origin https://github.com/flip/ped.git
   122	installer_refuses "refuses when core.hooksPath is set" "core.hooksPath is set" \
   123	  sh -c 'git remote add origin https://github.com/arch/ive.git && git config core.hooksPath .hooks'
   124	installer_refuses "refuses to overwrite a foreign pre-push hook" "is not this guard" \
   125	  sh -c 'git remote add origin https://github.com/arch/ive.git && printf "#!/bin/sh\nexit 0\n" > .git/hooks/pre-push'
   126	installer_refuses "refuses an origin that is not on GitHub" "not a GitHub repository" \
   127	  git remote add origin "$work/somewhere.git"

   129	# The installer's own lookup must be pinned too: a copy without --hostname must

   131	d="$work/inst-unpinned"

   138	if cmp -s "$INSTALL_SRC" "$d/tools/install-push-guard.sh"; then
   139	  fail "control: the installer mutation did not apply"

   156	ref() { printf 'refs/heads/main %s refs/heads/main %s\n' "$1" "$ZERO"; }

   158	# One case: <want 0|1> <needle or -> <url> <stdin> <repo> <PATH> <table> [GH_HOST]
   159	case_result() {
