   161	  local out rc
   162	  out=$(cd "$repo" && printf '%s' "$input" | env ${ghhost:+GH_HOST=$ghhost} PATH="$path" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$table" "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push" origin "$url" 2>&1)
   163	  rc=$?
   164	  if [ "$want" = 0 ]; then
   165	    [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]
   166	  else
   167	    [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && { [ "$needle" = - ] || printf '%s' "$out" | grep -qF "$needle"; }
   168	  fi
   169	}
   170	
   171	NOT_ARCHIVE="is not the archive"
   172	NOT_GITHUB="is not a repository on github.com"
   173	CASES=(
   174	  "A1|0|-|https://github.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   175	  "A2|0|-|git@github.com:arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   176	  "A3|0|-|https://GITHUB.COM/Arch/Ive.git/|PRIV"
   177	  "A4|0|-|ssh://ssh.github.com:443/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   178	  "A5|0|-|https://github.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV|GHHOST"
   179	  "R1|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV"
   180	  "R2|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|git@github.com:pub/lic.git|PRIV"
   181	  "R3|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|ssh://git@github.com/pub/lic|PRIV"
   182	  "R4|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/PUB/LIC.git/|PRIV"
   183	  "R5|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://git@github.com/pub/lic|PRIV"
   184	  "R6|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic//|PRIV"
   185	  "R7|1|$NOT_GITHUB|$work/public.git|PRIV"
   186	  "R8|1|$NOT_GITHUB|file://$work/public.git|PRIV"
   187	  "R9|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://github.com.example.org/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   188	  "R10|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://example.org/github.com/arch/ive|PRIV"
   189	  "R11|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://gitlab.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV"
   190	  "R12|1|$NOT_GITHUB|https://github.com/arch/ive/extra|PRIV"
   191	  "R13|1|could not be read|https://github.com/no/such|PRIV"
   192	  "R14|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|DELETE"
   193	  "R15|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|ORPH"
   194	  "R16|1|gh is not on PATH|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV|NOGH"
   195	  "R17|1|is not private|https://github.com/arch/ive.git|PRIV|FLIPPED"
   196	  "R18|1|not configured|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV|UNCONF"
   197	  "R19|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|PRIV|GHHOST"
   198	  "R20|1|$NOT_ARCHIVE|https://github.com/pub/lic.git|EMPTY"
   199	)
   200	CASE_COUNT=${#CASES[@]}
   201	
   202	# Runs every case; prints the names that fail, one per line.
   203	run_cases() {
   204	  local c name want needle url what mode input repo path table ghhost
   205	  for c in "${CASES[@]}"; do
   206	    IFS='|' read -r name want needle url what mode <<<"$c"
   207	    repo=$P path=$PATH_GH table=$TABLE ghhost=
   208	    case "$what" in
   209	      PRIV) input=$(ref "$PRIV") ;;
   210	      ORPH) input=$(ref "$ORPH") ;;
   211	      DELETE) input=$(printf '(delete) %s refs/heads/old %s\n' "$ZERO" "$PRIV") ;;
   212	      EMPTY) input= ;;
   213	    esac
   214	    case "${mode-}" in
   215	      NOGH) path=$PATH_NO_GH ;;
   216	      FLIPPED) table=$TABLE_FLIPPED ;;
   217	      UNCONF) repo=$U ;;
   218	      GHHOST) ghhost=ghe.example.invalid ;;
   219	    esac
   220	    case_result "$want" "$needle" "$url" "$input" "$repo" "$path" "$table" "$ghhost" || echo "$name"
   221	  done
   222	}
   223	
   224	echo
   225	echo "The hook, ${CASE_COUNT} cases:"
   226	failed_cases=$(run_cases)
   227	if [ -z "$failed_cases" ]; then
   228	  pass "all ${CASE_COUNT} cases"
   229	else
   230	  for n in $failed_cases; do fail "case $n"; done
   231	fi
   232	
   233	echo
   234	echo "Through git itself:"
   235	G init -q --bare "$work/public.git"
   236	pushes() { # <label> <git push args...>: the push must fail and deliver nothing
   237	  local label=$1
   238	  shift
   239	  if (cd "$P" && PATH="$PATH_GH" FAKE_GH_TABLE="$TABLE" G push -q "$@" 2>/dev/null); then
   240	    fail "$label"
   241	  elif [ -n "$(G -C "$work/public.git" for-each-ref)" ]; then
   242	    fail "$label (but refs arrived)"
   243	  else
   244	    pass "$label"
   245	  fi
   246	}
   247	pushes "a real push of the private history is refused, and nothing arrives" "$work/public.git" main
   248	pushes "a commit without the private history is refused too" "$work/public.git" clean
   249	pushes "a dry run is refused, so the guard can be proved against a real repository safely" --dry-run "$work/public.git" main
   250	# REVIEW-057 R2-1: a replacement ref hid the private ancestry from the old hook
   251	# while git sent the original history.
   252	G -C "$P" replace "$PRIV" "$ORPH"
   253	pushes "a push behind a git replace ref is refused, and nothing arrives" "$work/public.git" main
   254	G -C "$P" replace -d "$PRIV" >/dev/null
   255	
   256	# --- mutants: each removes one protection, and the named case must catch it
   257	echo
   258	echo "Mutants — each must be caught by the case named:"
   259	# Four parallel arrays, because several patterns contain `|`.
   260	M_MUST=() M_LABEL=() M_FROM=() M_TO=()
   261	mutant() { M_MUST+=("$1"); M_LABEL+=("$2"); M_FROM+=("$3"); M_TO+=("$4"); }
   262	mutant R1 "repository id not compared" '[ "$id" = "$archive_id" ]' 'true'
   263	mutant R7 "a destination off github.com allowed" '|| refuse "the destination is not a repository on github.com"' '|| exit 0'
   264	mutant R9 "host matched loosely" 'github.com | www.github.com | ssh.github.com) ;;' '*github.com*) ;;'
   265	mutant R12 "extra path segments accepted" "*/*/* | /* | */ | '') return 1 ;;" "/* | */ | '') return 1 ;;"
   266	mutant R13 "fail-open when GitHub cannot be read" '|| refuse "repos/$target could not be read from github.com"' '|| exit 0'
   267	mutant R16 "a missing gh ignored" '|| refuse "gh is not on PATH, so the destination cannot be checked"' '|| true'
   268	mutant R17 "archive's visibility not checked" '[ "$private" = "true" ]' 'true'
   269	mutant R18 "unconfigured clone allowed" '|| refuse "the guard is not configured in this clone; run make install-push-guard"' '|| exit 0'
   270	mutant R19 "lookup not pinned to github.com" 'gh api --hostname github.com "repos/$target"' 'gh api "repos/$target"'
   271	MUTANT_COUNT=${#M_MUST[@]}
   272	if [ -n "$failed_cases" ]; then
   273	  fail "no mutant evaluated: the real hook fails cases, so a mutant's failures would prove nothing"
   274	  M_MUST=()
   275	fi
   276	for i in "${!M_MUST[@]}"; do
   277	  must=${M_MUST[$i]} label=${M_LABEL[$i]}
   278	  for repo in "$P" "$U"; do
   279	    cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push"
   280	    FROM=${M_FROM[$i]} TO=${M_TO[$i]} perl -0pi -e 's/\Q$ENV{FROM}\E/$ENV{TO}/' "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push"
   281	  done
   282	  if cmp -s "$HOOK_SRC" "$P/.git/hooks/pre-push"; then
   283	    fail "mutant '$label' did not apply — the pattern no longer matches the hook"
   284	    continue
   285	  fi
   286	  caught=$(run_cases)
   287	  if printf '%s\n' "$caught" | grep -qx "$must"; then
   288	    pass "'$label' is caught by $must"
   289	  else
   290	    fail "'$label' is NOT caught by $must (cases failing: $(printf '%s' "$caught" | tr '\n' ' '))"
   291	  fi
   292	done
   293	for repo in "$P" "$U"; do cp "$HOOK_SRC" "$repo/.git/hooks/pre-push"; done
   294	
   295	echo
   296	echo "What this does not check:"
   297	echo "  1. git push --no-verify, which skips every hook; a clone the guard was never"
   298	echo "     installed in; and core.hooksPath set after the install."
   299	echo "  2. Real GitHub. gh is faked here. make install-push-guard reads the real"
   300	echo "     archive, and a git push --dry-run to a real public repository proves the"
   301	echo "     refusal without sending anything."
   302	echo
   303	if [ "$FAILED" -eq 0 ]; then
   304	  printf 'check-push-guard: verdict=PASS cases=%d mutants=%d exit=0\n' "$CASE_COUNT" "$MUTANT_COUNT"
   305	  exit 0
   306	fi
   307	printf 'check-push-guard: verdict=FAIL failures=%d exit=1\n' "$FAILED"
   308	exit 1

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * The third-party inventory: which tracked files reproduce upstream source or
     3	 * specification text, from which project, under which licence.
     4	 *
     5	 * Decision request phase 1, D3 and item 5 step 1 (`REVIEW-057` R1-1). The
     6	 * `NOTICE` is written from this tool's output, not from memory: the first
     7	 * draft named one patch and one harness, and the records quoted a whole
     8	 * BSD-3-Clause file nobody had counted.
     9	 *
    10	 * Method. Each upstream source is a CORPUS fetched at a stated version. A
    11	 * tracked file reproduces a corpus where:
    12	 *   - CODE: four or more substantive lines in a row (gaps of up to two
    13	 *     non-substantive lines allowed) each appear verbatim, after trimming and
    14	 *     collapsing whitespace, somewhere in that corpus; or
    15	 *   - CODE, across lines: a run of 24-token windows appears in that corpus, so
    16	 *     code joined onto one line or reflowed by a formatter is still found; or
    17	 *   - PROSE (specification and documentation corpora only): a run of 12
    18	 *     consecutive words appears in that corpus, so a quotation that was
    19	 *     re-wrapped or inlined is still found.
    20	 * Before either code pass, a tracked line loses a line-number prefix of the
    21	 * kind `cat -n`, `nl` and `grep -n` print: the first version of this tool
    22	 * matched lines exactly and missed every numbered quotation in the review
    23	 * transcripts (`REVIEW-057` R2-5).
    24	 * A substantive line has at least 20 characters after normalising and at
    25	 * least one letter. The thresholds are a choice, printed in the output.
    26	 *
    27	 *   bun run tools/inventory-third-party.ts            # fetch, compare, write
    28	 *   bun run tools/inventory-third-party.ts --check    # also fail if NOTICE omits a project found
    29	 *
    30	 * Needs network for the GitHub corpora; the crates come from the local cargo
    31	 * registry and the npm packages from node_modules. Not part of `make ci`.
    32	 */
    33	import { spawnSync } from "bun";
    34	import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
    35	import { join, relative } from "node:path";
    36	
    37	const ROOT = join(import.meta.dir, "..");
    38	const CACHE = process.env.WCONF_INVENTORY_CACHE ?? join(process.env.TMPDIR ?? "/tmp", "wconf-third-party-corpora");
    39	const OUT = join(ROOT, "docs/third-party/INVENTORY.md");
    40	const MIN_RUN = 4;
    41	const MAX_GAP = 2;
    42	const MIN_LINE = 20;
    43	const SHINGLE = 12;
    44	const TOKEN_WINDOW = 24;
    45	const MIN_TOKEN_RUN = 12;

    47	type Corpus = { name: string; version: string; licence: string; dir: string; prose: boolean };
    48	
    49	function run(cmd: string[], cwd?: string): string {
    50	  const r = spawnSync(cmd, { cwd, stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe" });
    51	  if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`${cmd.join(" ")} failed: ${r.stderr.toString().trim()}`);
    52	  return r.stdout.toString();
    53	}
    54	
    55	/** A shallow fetch of one ref of a GitHub repository into the cache. */
    56	function github(repo: string, ref: string): string {
    57	  const dir = join(CACHE, `${repo.replace("/", "__")}@${ref}`);
    58	  if (existsSync(join(dir, ".done"))) return dir;
    59	  mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    60	  run(["git", "init", "-q"], dir);
    61	  run(["git", "fetch", "-q", "--depth", "1", `https://github.com/${repo}.git`, ref], dir);
    62	  run(["git", "-c", "advice.detachedHead=false", "checkout", "-q", "FETCH_HEAD"], dir);
    63	  writeFileSync(join(dir, ".done"), "");
    64	  return dir;
    65	}
    66	
    67	/**
    68	 * Only the files of a repository whose path matches, fetched one by one: the
    69	 * dev portal is far too large to clone for the pages that concern this project.
    70	 */
    71	function pathsMatching(repo: string, ref: string, re: RegExp): Corpus {
    72	  const dir = join(CACHE, `${repo.replace("/", "__")}@${ref}-${re.source}`);
    73	  if (!existsSync(join(dir, ".done"))) {
    74	    mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    75	    const tree = JSON.parse(run(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/git/trees/${ref}?recursive=1`]));
    76	    if (tree.truncated) throw new Error(`${repo} tree listing is truncated; the path filter would miss files`);
    77	    for (const e of tree.tree) {
    78	      if (e.type !== "blob" || !re.test(e.path) || !TEXT.test(e.path)) continue;
    79	      const body = run(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/contents/${encodeURI(e.path)}?ref=${ref}`, "-H", "Accept: application/vnd.github.raw"]);
    80	      const out = join(dir, e.path);
    81	      mkdirSync(join(out, ".."), { recursive: true });
    82	      writeFileSync(out, body);
    83	    }
    84	    writeFileSync(join(dir, ".done"), "");
    85	  }
    86	  // The root licence file, so the NOTICE check can compare LICENSES/ against it.
    87	  if (!existsSync(join(dir, "LICENSE"))) {
    88	    writeFileSync(join(dir, "LICENSE"), run(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/contents/LICENSE?ref=${ref}`, "-H", "Accept: application/vnd.github.raw"]));
    89	  }
    90	  return { name: repo, version: `${ref}, paths matching ${re}`, licence: githubLicence(repo), dir, prose: true };
    91	}
    92	
    93	function githubLicence(repo: string): string {
    94	  const r = spawnSync(["gh", "api", `repos/${repo}/license`, "--jq", ".license.spdx_id"], { stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe" });
    95	  return r.exitCode === 0 ? r.stdout.toString().trim() : "no licence file found";
    96	}
    97	
    98	function crates(): Corpus[] {
    99	  const lock = readFileSync(join(ROOT, "signer/Cargo.lock"), "utf8");
   100	  const registry = join(process.env.HOME ?? "", ".cargo/registry/src");
   101	  const indexes = existsSync(registry) ? readdirSync(registry).map((d) => join(registry, d)) : [];
   102	  const out: Corpus[] = [];
   103	  for (const m of lock.matchAll(/\[\[package\]\]\nname = "([^"]+)"\nversion = "([^"]+)"\nsource = "registry/g)) {
   104	    const [, name, version] = m;
   105	    const dir = indexes.map((i) => join(i, `${name}-${version}`)).find((d) => existsSync(d));
   106	    if (!dir) {
   107	      out.push({ name: `crate ${name}`, version, licence: "NOT FOUND LOCALLY — not compared", dir: "", prose: false });
   108	      continue;
   109	    }
   110	    const toml = readFileSync(join(dir, "Cargo.toml"), "utf8");
   111	    const licence = toml.match(/^license\s*=\s*"([^"]+)"/m)?.[1] ?? "see crate";
   112	    out.push({ name: `crate ${name}`, version, licence, dir, prose: false });
   113	  }
   114	  return out;
   115	}
   116	
   117	function npmPackages(): Corpus[] {
   118	  const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(ROOT, "package.json"), "utf8"));
   119	  const names = [...Object.keys(pkg.dependencies ?? {}), ...Object.keys(pkg.devDependencies ?? {})];
   120	  return names.map((name) => {
   121	    const dir = join(ROOT, "node_modules", name);
   122	    if (!existsSync(join(dir, "package.json"))) return { name: `npm ${name}`, version: "?", licence: "NOT INSTALLED — not compared", dir: "", prose: false };
   123	    const p = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, "package.json"), "utf8"));
   124	    return { name: `npm ${name}`, version: p.version, licence: p.license ?? "see package", dir, prose: false };
   125	  });
   126	}

   151	const norm = (s: string) => s.trim().replace(/\s+/g, " ");
   152	const substantive = (s: string) => s.length >= MIN_LINE && /[A-Za-z]/.test(s);
   153	const TEXT = /\.(c|h|cc|cpp|hpp|ipp|rs|ts|tsx|js|mjs|cjs|jsx|md|txt|py|sh|toml|json|yml|yaml|cmake|in|patch|html|css)$|(^|\/)(CMakeLists\.txt|Makefile|LICENSE[^/]*)$/i;
   154	
   155	function walk(dir: string, into: string[] = []): string[] {
   156	  for (const e of readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   157	    if (e.name === ".git" || e.name === "node_modules") continue;
   158	    const p = join(dir, e.name);
   159	    if (e.isDirectory()) walk(p, into);
   160	    else if (e.isFile() && TEXT.test(p) && statSync(p).size < 2_000_000) into.push(p);
   161	  }
   162	  return into;
   163	}
   164	
   165	function words(s: string): string[] {
   166	  return s.toLowerCase().replace(/[`*_>#|\[\]()]/g, " ").split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean);
   167	}
   168	
   169	/**
   170	 * A line-number prefix, as `cat -n` and `nl` (`   12\t`), numbered viewers
   171	 * (`12→`, `12|`), and `grep -n` with or without a file name (`src/x.c:12:`,
   172	 * `12:`) print it. Stripping too much can only add matches, which is the safe
   173	 * direction for a notice.
   174	 */
   175	const PREFIXES = [/^\s*\d+\t/, /^\s*\d+\s*[→│|]\s?/, /^[\w.\/+@-]*\.\w+[:-]\d+[:-]/, /^\d+[:-]/];
   176	function stripPrefix(l: string): string {
   177	  for (const re of PREFIXES) if (re.test(l)) return l.replace(re, "");
   178	  return l;
   179	}
   180	
   181	/**
   182	 * Identifiers and numbers only. Punctuation tokens made box-drawing lines and
   183	 * operator-heavy idioms match across projects, and comment markers kept a
   184	 * licence header in a source file from matching the same licence's file.
   185	 */
   186	function tokens(text: string): { tok: string[]; line: number[] } {
   187	  const tok: string[] = [], line: number[] = [];
   188	  text.split("\n").forEach((l, i) => {
   189	    for (const m of l.matchAll(/[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*|\d\w*/g)) { tok.push(m[0]); line.push(i); }
   190	  });
   191	  return { tok, line };
   192	}
   193	
   194	/** A window of mostly names, not numbers: a table of published constants is no quotation. */
   195	function windowValid(tok: string[], i: number): boolean {
   196	  let names = 0;
   197	  for (let k = i; k < i + TOKEN_WINDOW; k++) if (/^[A-Za-z_]/.test(tok[k])) names++;
   198	  return names * 2 >= TOKEN_WINDOW;
   199	}
   200	const MANIFEST = /(^|\/)(Cargo\.toml(\.orig)?|package\.json|[^/]*\.lock)$/;
   201	
   202	/** A 53-bit hash of one token window, from two independent 32-bit hashes. */
   203	function windowHash(tok: string[], i: number): number {
   204	  const s = tok.slice(i, i + TOKEN_WINDOW).join(" ");
   205	  return (Bun.hash.crc32(s) * 0x200000) + (Bun.hash.adler32(s) & 0x1fffff);
   206	}
   207	
   208	/** A project is a repository, crate or package, whatever its version. */
   209	const project = (c: Corpus) => c.name;
   210	
   211	// line -> the projects whose corpus contains it; and the lines of licence files.
   212	const LINES = new Map<string, Set<string>>();
   213	const LICENCE_LINES = new Set<string>();
   214	const SHINGLES = new Map<string, Set<string>>();
   215	const VERSIONS = new Map<string, Set<string>>();
   216	// Token windows: the tracked files' hashes, and for each one found in a corpus
   217	// the projects that hold it. Only tracked hashes are kept, so a corpus is
   218	// streamed rather than stored.
   219	const TRACKED_HASHES = new Set<number>();
   220	const TOKEN_HITS = new Map<number, Set<string>>();
   221	// Windows found in any project's licence file: never evidence of a quotation.
   222	const LICENCE_WINDOWS = new Set<number>();
   223	// Each project's root licence files, for the NOTICE check.
   224	const LICENCE_FILES = new Map<string, Buffer[]>();
   225	
   226	function index(c: Corpus): number {
   227	  let n = 0;
   228	  for (const f of walk(c.dir)) {
   229	    const text = readFileSync(f, "utf8");
   230	    const isLicence = /(^|\/)(LICEN[CS]E|COPYING|NOTICE)[^/]*$/i.test(f);
   231	    for (const l of text.split("\n")) {
   232	      const s = norm(l);
   233	      if (!substantive(s)) continue;
   234	      if (isLicence) LICENCE_LINES.add(s);
   235	      let set = LINES.get(s);
   236	      if (!set) LINES.set(s, (set = new Set()));
   237	      set.add(project(c));
   238	      n++;
   239	    }
   240	    if (isLicence && !relative(c.dir, f).includes("/")) {
   241	      if (!LICENCE_FILES.has(project(c))) LICENCE_FILES.set(project(c), []);
   242	      LICENCE_FILES.get(project(c))!.push(readFileSync(f));
   243	    }
   244	    // Licence texts are left to the line pass, which classifies them; minified
   245	    // files carry no quotable structure and cost most of the time.
   246	    // Minified files carry no quotable structure and cost most of the time;
   247	    // manifests are dependency lists every crate shares, and are left to the
   248	    // line pass.
   249	    if (!/^.{2000,}$/m.test(text) && !MANIFEST.test(f)) {
   250	      const { tok } = tokens(text);
   251	      for (let i = 0; i + TOKEN_WINDOW <= tok.length; i++) {
   252	        const h = windowHash(tok, i);
   253	        if (!TRACKED_HASHES.has(h)) continue;
   254	        if (isLicence) { LICENCE_WINDOWS.add(h); continue; }
   255	        let set = TOKEN_HITS.get(h);
   256	        if (!set) TOKEN_HITS.set(h, (set = new Set()));
   257	        set.add(project(c));
   258	      }
   259	    }
   260	    if (c.prose && /\.md$/i.test(f)) {
   261	      const w = words(text);
   262	      for (let i = 0; i + SHINGLE <= w.length; i++) {
   263	        const k = w.slice(i, i + SHINGLE).join(" ");
   264	        let set = SHINGLES.get(k);
   265	        if (!set) SHINGLES.set(k, (set = new Set()));
   266	        set.add(project(c));
   267	      }
   268	    }
   269	  }
   270	  let v = VERSIONS.get(project(c));
   271	  if (!v) VERSIONS.set(project(c), (v = new Set()));
   272	  v.add(c.version);
   273	  return n;
   274	}

   181	
   182	**Recommended and ruled: the public repository is `nandanito/xrpl-confidential-positions`,
   183	and the private one is renamed `xrpl-confidential-positions-private` first.** That
   184	is the pattern the EVM study already uses. `nandanito/evm-confidential-positions-private`
   185	is private and `nandanito/evm-confidential-positions` is public, created the next
   186	day (both read with `gh repo view` on 2026-10-18). The private repository holds the
   187	name today, so the name has to be freed before the public one can take it. The
   188	personal account matches the blog and the LinkedIn post. An organisation adds
   189	nothing for a single-maintainer research artifact.
   190	
   191	**The rename, read on GitHub's rename page on 2026-10-18.** After a rename, *"all
   192	existing information, with the exception of project site URLs, is automatically
   193	redirected to the new name."* If a new repository then takes the old name,
   194	*"redirects to the renamed repository will no longer work."* Here that is what is
   195	wanted: the canonical name should land on the public repository. Every existing
   196	clone needs `git remote set-url origin` to the new name. The page also says
   197	*"GitHub will not redirect calls to an action hosted by a renamed repository"*;
   198	this repository hosts no action.
   199	
   200	**Ruled, with one addition: the local folder keeps its name.** This checkout stays
   201	at `…/xrpl-confidential-positions`. Claude Code's memory is keyed by the folder's
   202	path, and the owner found that renaming the EVM study's folder broke both its Claude
   203	Code and its Codex sessions. Only its `origin` changes, to `xrpl-confidential-positions-private`. The
   204	public repository's working copy is a separate folder, the step 4 export.
   205

   207	PRIVATE history**, and one mistake publishes all of it: the history that
   208	`--strict-history` fails on 1,045 personal-path hits, and the 36 commits behind the
   209	withheld rows. Once the public repository takes the old name, a stale `origin` in
   210	any clone stops redirecting to the archive and points at the public repository
   211	instead. **So every private clone gets the push guard** (`make install-push-guard`),
   212	and the public URL is never added as a remote here. ⚠ This first said the guard
   213	would refuse URLs *"naming `nandanito/xrpl-confidential-positions` without the
   214	`-private` suffix"*. It was built as an allowlist by repository id instead, and
   215	what it does and does not close is D8.

   287	### D8. What keeps the private history out of the public repository
   288	
   289	**Context.** `REVIEW-057` R1-3, upheld in part. A `pre-push` hook is not a
   290	boundary: the leg measured that `--no-verify`, a fresh clone and `core.hooksPath`
   291	each skip it. The plan, as frozen, described the hook as if it closed the route.
   292	Since then the guard has been built as an allowlist rather than a URL pattern: a
   293	push that carries the private history may go only to the archive, identified by
   294	its GitHub repository id (`tools/git-hooks/pre-push`, `make check-push-guard`, in
   295	`make ci`). It is installed in this clone and proved with dry runs. ⚠ **It stops
   296	mistakes in a clone it is installed in, and nothing else.**
   297	
   298	| option | cost | what it buys |
   299	|---|---|---|
   300	| **A. The guard, plus an inventory of every private clone** *(recommended)* | step 8 lists every clone on every machine, including any cloud session's checkout, and sets each one's `origin` and installs the guard | closes the realistic route, a stale `origin` after the public repository takes the old name. ⚠ **Residual, named**: a clone the inventory missed, or `--no-verify` |
   301	| B. A, plus credential isolation | day-to-day `git` and `gh` credentials scoped so they cannot write the public repository, and a separate fine-grained token kept only in the export folder; every later public change goes through that folder or the web interface | the leg's recommendation: a real boundary on this machine, whatever the clone |
   302	| C. A, plus a server-side ruleset on the public repository | refs may be created, updated or deleted only by merging a pull request, so even the owner's changes arrive from a fork; ⚠ the ruleset's availability on this account is not verified | closes the route for every clone and every credential |
   303	
   304	**Ruled 2026-10-18 by the owner: A.** The inventory began the same day, on this
   305	machine only: every repository under the home directory's project folders and the
   306	temporary directories, searched to depth 7, was tested for the private history's
   307	root commit `b71638c`. Seven hold it. **This checkout** has the guard. **Six are
   308	scratch clones** in the temporary directory, from `REVIEW-056`'s and `REVIEW-057`'s
   309	legs and an earlier CI rehearsal; none has a GitHub remote, so none can make the
   310	stale-`origin` push. **They were deleted the same day at the owner's word, and the
   311	owner confirmed there are no other clones**, on other machines or in cloud
   312	sessions. A second search then found the private history in this checkout only.
   313	
   314	**Why A.** The route that exists in practice is a clone whose `origin` still names
   315	the old repository, and the inventory and the guard close it. B and C close the
   316	rest at a standing cost to every later change, for a single-maintainer research
   317	repository. **If you want the boundary rather than the guard, B is the cheaper of
   318	the two.**
   319	
   320	## Item 5, the publication steps — every decision ruled 2026-10-18
   321	
   322	⚠ **Revised after `REVIEW-057`.** The frozen version promised *"one commit"* while
   323	steps 4 to 6 made four, and a log derived from git history cannot name the commit
   324	that contains it, so squashing them fails the revision-log gate (R1-6). It also
   325	gated the tree with history scanning off and edited the workflow after the gate
   326	(R1-7), required a check named `ci` that the workflow never emits (R1-8), and saw
   327	the first Linux CI run only after going public (R1-9).
   328	
   329	1. **Apply D3.** Run the inventory of quoted third-party source, complete `NOTICE`
   330	   from it, and add a line pointing to it in the README's *Licence* section.
   331	   **Done 2026-10-18**: see D3.
   332	2. Pin the workflow's actions by commit SHA. Commit both on the stack and re-run
   333	   `make ci`. **Done 2026-10-18**: `actions/checkout` at `11d5960` (`v4.4.0`) and
   334	   `oven-sh/setup-bun` at `0c5077e` (`v2.2.0`), each the commit its tag named that
   335	   day. ⚠ The workflow cannot run locally, so step 9's staging run is its first test.
   336	3. Merge the stack to `main` and push it to the private origin.
   337	4. ~~Run D1's round, if ruled, and fix what it upholds.~~ **Done 2026-10-18**
   338	   (`REVIEW-056e`).
   339	5. **Export, and make the first of exactly two commits.** `git archive` the
   340	   merged `main` into a fresh repository. Before committing, copy
   341	   `docs/CLAIMS-REVISIONS.md` to `docs/CLAIMS-REVISIONS-ARCHIVE.md` (D2), and add to
   342	   the README's first screen: *"Public history starts at publication. This
   343	   repository was developed privately from 2026-08-16 and published on DATE as two
   344	   commits: the tree, and its revision log regenerated from this history. The commit
   345	   hashes cited in `CLAIMS.md` and `docs/reviews/` refer to that private archive and
   346	   do not resolve here. Issues #1 to #13 were copied from the archive in order, so
   347	   the issue numbers cited here resolve; the ten closed ones are stubs."* Commit.
   348	   Confirm with `git ls-files` that `docs/upstream-observed-WITHHELD.md` and
   349	   `docs/evidence/f12-emsdk-2026-10-18/logs-unscrubbed/` are absent and `NOTICE` is
   350	   present.
   351	6. **The second commit, artifact-only:** run `bun run tools/build-claims-revisions.ts`
   352	   and commit the regenerated log. Rehearsed on 2026-10-18 at `bceea15`: two commits,
   353	   the oracle not run because history starts at publication, and the check passed.
   354	7. **Run the phase 1 gate on the export, with the worktree clean**:
   355	   `bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts --repo <export> --strict-history`, **without
   356	   `--rev`**, so it scans both commits' history as well as the tree (`--rev` turns
   357	   history off), and fails on personal data in that history rather than reporting
   358	   it: both commits are published (`REVIEW-057` R2-7). Then `make ci` there. Record `git rev-parse HEAD`. ⚠ **Any later change to the export
   359	   goes back to step 5.**
   360	8. **Apply D4 and D8.** Rename the private repository to
   361	   `xrpl-confidential-positions-private`. In every private clone on the inventory,
   362	   run `git remote set-url origin`, confirm with `git ls-remote origin`, and run
   363	   `make install-push-guard`.
   364	9. **Stage it privately.** Create `nandanito/xrpl-confidential-positions` as a
   365	   private repository and push **only the recorded commit**:
   366	   `git push <url> <recorded-sha>:refs/heads/main`. Confirm with `git ls-remote`
   367	   that it holds that one ref and nothing else. **Wait for the first CI run there to
   368	   pass**, because no Linux run has happened yet. Note the check name it emits; the
   369	   job is `checks`. Then apply D7, as specified under it.
   370	10. **Make it public**, then set private vulnerability reporting on, which
   371	    `SECURITY.md` and `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` rely on, and branch protection requiring
   372	    the check step 9 recorded. Clone it fresh and re-run step 7's gate on that clone.

     1	# Third-party inventory
     2	
     3	⚠⚠ **GENERATED. DO NOT HAND-EDIT.** Rebuild with `bun run tools/inventory-third-party.ts`.
     4	
     5	Which tracked files reproduce upstream source or specification text. `NOTICE` is
     6	written from this file. Method: code is **4 or more substantive lines in a row**,
     7	with gaps of up to 2, each verbatim in a corpus after collapsing whitespace, and
     8	is attributed to every project holding 4 or more of the run's lines; prose is
     9	**a run of 12 consecutive words**, searched in the specification and documentation
    10	corpora only. A substantive line has 20 or more characters and a letter. A run
    11	is **licence text** when 80% of its lines come from licence files, and
    12	**shared boilerplate** when more than 3 projects hold it; neither is
    13	attributed, and only lines held by 3 projects or fewer count toward
    14	attribution. ⚠ **What this cannot see**: a quotation shorter than those thresholds, a
    15	paraphrase, and a source that is not a corpus below. Sources the records are known to
    16	cite and that are **not** corpora: the Trail of Bits report and the Dore and Cenk papers,
    17	which are PDFs under `docs/references/` and not in version control;
    18	`bitcoin-core/secp256k1`; and web pages quoted a sentence at a time, such as GitHub's
    19	documentation; and diff-prefixed (`+`/`-`) quotations inside Markdown, outside a
    20	`.patch` file.
    21	
    22	**What is compared.** On the tracked side, every file `git ls-files` lists with a text
    23	extension (592 files), except lockfiles, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `LICENSES/`
    24	and this directory; lines lose a line-number prefix first. On the upstream side, 108
    25	corpora are configured in 105 projects:
    26	**94 were compared and 14 were unavailable**, listed
    27	at the end. A corpus's files over 2 MB, and minified files, are not tokenised.
    28

   264	
   265	**What is published, exactly** (`REVIEW-057` R1-4, R2-10). A copy is the title and
   266	a body, and nothing else: no comments, attachments or labels. Both are written to a
   267	frozen manifest, one file per issue holding its number, its title and its body. The
   268	owner reads the three open bodies for any restatement of a withheld row (the id scan
   269	cannot see a paraphrase), the manifest is swept, and each issue is created from its
   270	own file, `--title` and `--body-file` both read from it. ⚠ The first version swept
   271	the bodies only, while the titles were published too. Nothing is re-fetched from
   272	the archive at creation time.
   273	
   274	A stub carries the original title and says: *"A stub. This was issue #N in the
   275	private archive, opened DATE and closed DATE. Its discussion stays there."* ⚠ **It
   276	said here that *"the outcome is in the document that cites it"*, and that is not
   277	true of every citation** (R1-5): `REVIEW-013`'s request cites the re-weigh while it
   278	was still open, and its outcome is in `REVIEW-013c`. So a stub links its outcome
   279	only where a document is verified to record it, and otherwise says nothing about
   280	one.
   281	
   282	⚠ **The issues have to be created before anything else can take a number.** The
   283	public repository is created private and made public only after them, Discussions
   284	stay off until then, and each number GitHub returns is checked as it is created,
   285	stopping at the first mismatch. Being private does not reserve a number by itself.
   286	
   287	### D8. What keeps the private history out of the public repository
   288	
   289	**Context.** `REVIEW-057` R1-3, upheld in part. A `pre-push` hook is not a
   290	boundary: the leg measured that `--no-verify`, a fresh clone and `core.hooksPath`
   291	each skip it. The plan, as frozen, described the hook as if it closed the route.
   292	Since then the guard has been built as an allowlist rather than a URL pattern: a
   293	push that carries the private history may go only to the archive, identified by
   294	its GitHub repository id (`tools/git-hooks/pre-push`, `make check-push-guard`, in
   295	`make ci`). It is installed in this clone and proved with dry runs. ⚠ **It stops
   296	mistakes in a clone it is installed in, and nothing else.**
   297	
   298	| option | cost | what it buys |
   299	|---|---|---|
   300	| **A. The guard, plus an inventory of every private clone** *(recommended)* | step 8 lists every clone on every machine, including any cloud session's checkout, and sets each one's `origin` and installs the guard | closes the realistic route, a stale `origin` after the public repository takes the old name. ⚠ **Residual, named**: a clone the inventory missed, or `--no-verify` |
   301	| B. A, plus credential isolation | day-to-day `git` and `gh` credentials scoped so they cannot write the public repository, and a separate fine-grained token kept only in the export folder; every later public change goes through that folder or the web interface | the leg's recommendation: a real boundary on this machine, whatever the clone |
   302	| C. A, plus a server-side ruleset on the public repository | refs may be created, updated or deleted only by merging a pull request, so even the owner's changes arrive from a fork; ⚠ the ruleset's availability on this account is not verified | closes the route for every clone and every credential |
   303	
   304	**Ruled 2026-10-18 by the owner: A.** The inventory began the same day, on this
   305	machine only: every repository under the home directory's project folders and the
   306	temporary directories, searched to depth 7, was tested for the private history's
   307	root commit `b71638c`. Seven hold it. **This checkout** has the guard. **Six are
   308	scratch clones** in the temporary directory, from `REVIEW-056`'s and `REVIEW-057`'s
   309	legs and an earlier CI rehearsal; none has a GitHub remote, so none can make the
   310	stale-`origin` push. **They were deleted the same day at the owner's word, and the
   311	owner confirmed there are no other clones**, on other machines or in cloud
   312	sessions. A second search then found the private history in this checkout only.
   313	
   314	**Why A.** The route that exists in practice is a clone whose `origin` still names
   315	the old repository, and the inventory and the guard close it. B and C close the
   316	rest at a standing cost to every later change, for a single-maintainer research
   317	repository. **If you want the boundary rather than the guard, B is the cheaper of
   318	the two.**

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	#
     3	# ci.sh — everything that runs WITHOUT the vendored build, in one list.
     4	#
     5	#   tools/ci.sh        # or: make ci
     6	#
     7	# GitHub Actions runs exactly this script (.github/workflows/ci.yml), and the
     8	# README's five-minute path tells a stranger to run it too. One list, so the
     9	# workflow, the README and CONTRIBUTING.md cannot drift apart on what is covered.
    10	#
    11	# ⚠ **What this does NOT run, and why** — printed at the end of every run too:
    12	#
    13	# * `cargo test` in `signer/`, `make check-test-support`, `make check-zeroize-bounds`
    14	#   and `make break-zeroize-lock`. All four compile the signer, which links a
    15	#   patched, vendored `mpt-crypto` that `tools/rebuild-env.sh` builds from pinned
    16	#   clones with Homebrew `openssl@3`. That is a macOS build this workflow does not
    17	#   perform.
    18	# * Five facility tests that start a real signer. They are SKIPPED by name, not
    19	#   passed: this script sets `WCONF_SKIP_SIGNER_TESTS=1`, and Bun reports them as
    20	#   `skip`.
    21	# * The known-value half of the secret sweep. `check-no-secrets-shapes` runs the
    22	#   shape classes only; the full `make check-no-secrets` needs the local secret
    23	#   store and runs on the owner's machine. Its verdict word says which it was.
    24	# * `check-filing` on the historical drafts. It runs on the template, as a positive
    25	#   control; 15 of the 20 committed drafts predate the rules it enforces.
    26	set -euo pipefail
    27	cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
    28	
    29	FAILED=()
    30	step() {
    31	  printf '\n=== %s\n' "$*"
    32	  if "$@"; then echo "--- ok: $*"; else echo "--- FAILED: $*"; FAILED+=("$*"); fi
    33	}
    34	
    35	step bunx tsc --noEmit
    36	step env WCONF_SKIP_SIGNER_TESTS=1 bun test facility web/api tools
    37	for gate in check-tables check-negative-claims check-personas check-blinding \
    38	            check-loopback check-web-readonly check-disclosure-phrases \
    39	            check-claims-index check-claims-topics check-claims-revisions \
    40	            check-emphasis check-vocabulary check-no-secrets-shapes \
    41	            check-push-guard; do
    42	  step make "$gate"
    43	done
    44	step make check-filing FILE=docs/upstream-filings/TEMPLATE.md
    45	
    46	cat <<'EOF'
    47	
    48	=== NOT RUN by this script (see its header): cargo test, check-test-support,
    49	    check-zeroize-bounds and break-zeroize-lock, which need the vendored build;
    50	    five facility tests that start a real signer, reported as skip; the
    51	    known-value half of the secret sweep, which needs the local store; and
    52	    check-filing on the historical drafts.
    53	EOF
    54	
    55	if [ "${#FAILED[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then
    56	  printf '\nci: FAILED %d step(s):\n' "${#FAILED[@]}"
    57	  printf '  %s\n' "${FAILED[@]}"
    58	  exit 1
    59	fi
    60	echo
    61	echo "ci: every step passed"

hook: PostToolUse Completed

   509	export function loadStore(paths: string[], env: Record<string, string | undefined> = process.env): Store {
   510	  const st: Store = { files: [], needles: [], secrets: 0, publicSkipped: 0 };
   511	  const files: string[] = [];
   512	  for (const p of paths) {
   513	    if (!existsSync(p)) continue;
   514	    const s = lstatSync(p);
   515	    if (s.isFile()) files.push(p);
   516	    else if (s.isDirectory()) for (const n of readdirSync(p)) if (n.includes(".json") && lstatSync(join(p, n)).isFile()) files.push(join(p, n));
   517	  }
   518	  // Counted by distinct VALUE: the same seed in two copies of state.json is one
   519	  // secret, and a count that doubled it would claim coverage it does not have.
   520	  const seen = new Set<string>();
   521	  const addSeed = (seed: string, label: string) => {
   522	    const d = decodeSeedLike(seed);
   523	    if (!d || !d.checksumOk) return;
   524	    if (publicEntropyRule(d.entropy)) { st.publicSkipped++; return; }
   525	    if (seen.has(seed)) return;
   526	    seen.add(seed);
   527	    st.secrets++;
   528	    st.needles.push(...needlesForSeed(seed, label));
   529	  };
   530	  const addHex = (v: string, label: string) => {
   531	    const k = v.toLowerCase();
   532	    if (seen.has(k)) return;
   533	    seen.add(k);
   534	    st.secrets++;
   535	    st.needles.push({ value: k, label, kind: "hex" });
   536	  };
   537	  for (const f of files) {
   538	    st.files.push(f);
   539	    const raw = readFileSync(f, "utf8");
   540	    const name = f.slice(f.lastIndexOf("/") + 1);
   541	    let json: unknown;
   542	    // ⚠ A malformed store file REFUSES (`REVIEW-056` S3-3). It used to fall back
   543	    // to scanning the raw text for seeds, which silently dropped every 64-hex
   544	    // scalar in it — the one shape only this class can find — while another
   545	    // file kept the store non-empty, so no refusal fired.
   546	    try { json = JSON.parse(raw); } catch (e) {
   547	      throw new Error(`store file ${f} is not valid JSON (${String(e).slice(0, 80)}); the known-value class cannot tell its secrets from its hashes, so it refuses rather than guess`);
   548	    }
   549	    const walk = (o: unknown, path: string) => {
   550	      if (Array.isArray(o)) o.forEach((v, i) => walk(v, `${path}[${i}]`));
   551	      else if (o && typeof o === "object") for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(o)) walk(v, `${path}.${k}`);
   552	      else if (typeof o === "string") {
   553	        const key = path.slice(path.lastIndexOf(".") + 1).replace(/\[\d+\]$/, "");

   556	      }
   557	    };
   558	    walk(json, "");
   559	  }
   560	  const envSk = env.WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK?.trim();
   561	  if (envSk && /^[0-9A-Fa-f]{64}$/.test(envSk)) addHex(envSk, "environment: WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK");
   562	  return st;

   364	|---|---|---|
   365	| **The key derivation is not published** | A holder with their master seed and **not this repository** cannot reconstruct their key — the entire benefit deriving buys. No code discharges it. ⚠ Treat the derivation as lock-in until it is published | issue #10 |
   366	| **Nothing has run under loopback** | Every Devnet read goes to the public cluster under a named risk acceptance, because a local node could not acquire the state tree on this hardware. Every run record says `underB15Conditions: false` | B-15, E-148, E-151 |
   367	| **The issuer snapshot is plaintext on disk** | `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` writes a curated set of decrypted demo balances to a file with mode `0600`. The web tier reads that file and no longer serves the figure (E-241), but any process running as the same user can read the file, and that is the adversary this project puts in scope. The removal has a falsifier test, verified by reverting it. `REVIEW-057`'s first round probed the running route with a synthetic snapshot on 2026-10-18 and found no figure served, which is a check of the code, not an adversarial review of the ruling. ⚠ **This row said until 2026-10-18 that the figure was also served on the wire**, which stopped on 2026-09-05 | E-212, E-241 |

   373	
   374	### Upstream
   375	
   376	**Eleven filings have gone out: seven on 2026-08-26, one on 2026-09-28 and three on 2026-10-18.** Every outcome below was re-surveyed at source on 2026-10-18, for this README ([`docs/evidence/upstream-survey-2026-10-18/`](docs/evidence/upstream-survey-2026-10-18/README.md)). Four of the first seven were fixed upstream in September and closed when the fix merged — the xrpl.org page by dev-portal PR #3916, XLS-0096 by PRs #604 and #628 — and this project did not notice for five weeks (E-316). [`rippled#8118`](https://github.com/XRPLF/rippled/issues/8118) was closed without comment the day it was filed (E-216), [`XRPL-Standards#611`](https://github.com/XRPLF/XRPL-Standards/issues/611) has had no reply, and the five `XRPLF/mpt-crypto` filings are open: one answered with a PR, one with a fix in review, three filed on 2026-10-18.
   377	
   378	The six then open were re-audited against upstream HEAD on 2026-09-01 and every claim still stood (E-216) — so on that evidence the defect was the **order of operations**, not the research: the adversarial review ran *after* submission, where it can only produce a public correction. One audit of six filings does not establish the process was sound in general, and ⚠ a register that does not re-survey its filings goes stale in the direction that flatters it (E-316).
   379	
   380	That order is now inverted: [`docs/UPSTREAM-FILING-PROTOCOL.md`](docs/UPSTREAM-FILING-PROTOCOL.md) rule 1 gates filing on a review by a **different session**. Filing is authorised per filing, per session, and on 2026-10-18 no authorisation was outstanding.
   381	
   382	**The register's ungraded section was withdrawn before publication.** [`UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md`](UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md) used to carry an *Observed — not yet verified, do not send* section: 33 rows of unverified observations about other people's work, captured under this project's rule that capture and verification are separate acts. **An adversarial rotation on publication found the heading insufficient** — it constrains a reader who reads it, not a search engine or a screenshot, and it risked the reputational effect of an accusation with a disclaimer of having verified it. The rows are withheld, not retracted, and rejoin the register the normal way: verified, graded, cited (`CLAIMS.md` E-240).
   383	
   384	**Nor are they in the public repository's history.** The public repository starts at publication, squashed into two commits: the tree, and its revision log regenerated from that history. The owner ruled the squash on 2026-09-22 (E-309). The private repository it was developed in is the archive of record, and it keeps the withheld rows along with the record of what this project believed and when it corrected itself. ⚠ **This paragraph said until 2026-10-18 that the rows would stay in public git history.** That was the owner's ruling of 2026-09-05 (E-240), and the squash ruling replaced it.
   385	
   386	**Found a defect here?** Open an issue on this repository. The filing protocol governs what this project sends *upstream* to XRPLF; it does not govern what you send us.
   387

   389	
   390	## Contributing, security and conduct
   391	
   392	[`CONTRIBUTING.md`](CONTRIBUTING.md) says what contributions are wanted and what a pull request must pass. [`SECURITY.md`](SECURITY.md) says how to report a vulnerability, and that this is a research artifact rather than production software. [`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`](CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md) is the Contributor Covenant.
   393	
   394	## Licence
   395	
   396	[Apache 2.0](LICENSE), for this project's own work. Material reproduced from third parties is not covered by it: a patch against `XRPLF/mpt-crypto`, which carries no licence, and quotations of other projects' source and specifications in the records. [`NOTICE`](NOTICE) lists that material, [`LICENSES/`](LICENSES/) carries the notices it requires, and [`docs/third-party/INVENTORY.md`](docs/third-party/INVENTORY.md) is the measured list it was written from.
   397	
   398	## Credit
   399	
   400	XLS-0096 is the work of Murat Cenk, Aanchal Malhotra, Ayo Akinyele, Peter Chen, Shawn Xie and Yinyi Qian, with the reference cryptography in [`XRPLF/mpt-crypto`](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto).
   401	
   402	The compact sigma protocol that gives the send proof its 192 bytes — with the twisted-ElGamal and Bulletproofs++ analyses behind several of the size decisions — is the work of **Tess Dore and Murat Cenk** at RippleX Research, published separately from XLS-0096. Cite them for it; copying the XLS-0096 author list alone leaves Dore out.
   403	
   404	The Confidential Transfer implementation was reviewed by **Trail of Bits** in April 2026. Several things this repository first recorded as findings turned out to be issues that review had already found and Ripple had already fixed, so check it before believing anything here is novel. ⚠ **A later revision of that report is recorded as an unobtainable source** (B-13) — this project has not read it. It was also not the only review: the Sherlock XRP Ledger Attackathon (contest 1260, April 2026) produced findings against the Confidential Transfer code that upstream issues cite by number — [`mpt-crypto#102`](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/102) cites Sherlock #2759, a MergeInbox finding — and this project could not obtain its findings by the routes it tried, so nothing here was checked against them (`CLAIMS.md` B-30). A third, a Zellic scan of `mpt-crypto` at `3baaaac`, is known only through the three findings [`mpt-crypto#147`](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/147) cites by identifier (`CLAIMS.md` B-31). **Of the three, only Trail of Bits' April report could be checked.**
   405

+- ⚠ **"reads public state" is false.** `Request::Status` returns the randomness ledger's **entry count**, and `signer/src/store.rs:753-756` commits a record *"on success **and** on any local failure before emission — a cancelled confirmation, a failed proof, an aborted attempt."* **Public ledger history cannot reveal a declined confirmation.** The tier also emitted the local socket path and every candidate filesystem path it probed, until E-241 on 2026-09-05. **Revision 1's defence — ~~*"activity metadata of exactly the kind the protocol leaves public"*~~, STRUCK — answered a question about SENSITIVITY with the premise written about PUBLICNESS (`CLAIMS.md` E-230).**
 - ⚠ **"holds no credential" is true and does not carry the weight put on it.** It is not the same property as **conveys nothing a credential produces**. The tier served `decrypted` confidential balances (until E-241, 2026-09-05), which no recipient could compute from public ledger state.
 - ⚠ **"grants no authority ⇒ no boundary moves" is invalid.** A trust boundary also governs **confidentiality**, **transport exposure** and **presentation integrity**. `CLAIMS.md` **E-209** is this project's own proof of the last one: the tier rendered a Docker-hosted Gitea's `303` as its own API response. **No authority moved; the display still lied.**

docs/reviews/REVIEW-016b-codex-step2-2026-08-19.md:9:`network_id` is read as `u64` and cast with `as u32`. A malformed or hostile `server_info` with `network_id = 4294967298` truncates to `2` and passes the “refuses any network_id but 2 before it reads anything else” guard. Similar truncation exists for ledger index, sequence, and balance version. Primary support: Rust `as u32` truncates; the code uses `as u32` before comparing to `DEVNET_NETWORK_ID`. Use `u32::try_from` and reject out-of-range values as malformed.

     1	/**
     2	 * xrpl-vectors.ts — the generator for `signer/tests/xrpl_vectors.rs`.
     3	 *
     4	 * Architecture §6 step 3 puts XRPL serialisation and signing inside the Rust
     5	 * signer. Nothing in this repository had ever produced XRPL bytes from Rust, so
     6	 * the encoder needed something to be wrong against. These vectors are it.
     7	 *
     8	 * **Committed under standing prohibition P-8:** a value whose generator is
     9	 * unavailable is not checkable. This file is the generator; `xrpl-vectors.json`
    10	 * is its output; the Rust test fails if the two disagree.
    11	 *
    12	 * Three independent implementations this project did not write produce every
    13	 * expected value here:
    14	 *
    15	 *   - `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`   — canonical serialisation. CLAIMS.md E-46
    16	 *                                      records it verified against a live
    17	 *                                      node's `server_definitions` across all
    18	 *                                      381 fields.
    19	 *   - `ripple-keypairs@2.0.0`       — family-seed derivation and ECDSA.
    20	 *   - `ripple-address-codec@5.0.1`  — base58check.
    21	 *
    22	 * Run it two ways:
    23	 *
    24	 *   bun tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts          # check, exit 1 on drift
    25	 *   bun tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts --write  # regenerate
    26	 *
    27	 * ⚠ **`--write` is how a wrong encoder gets blessed.** Regenerate only when the
    28	 * codec version changes, and say so in the commit. The whole value of a vector
    29	 * is that it was not written by the thing it tests.
    30	 *
    31	 * No network. No keys that matter. Devnet only, and this file does not submit.
    32	 */
    33	import { encode, encodeForSigning } from "ripple-binary-codec";
    34	import { deriveKeypair, deriveAddress, sign } from "ripple-keypairs";
    35	import { decodeAccountID, encodeSeed } from "ripple-address-codec";
    36	import { existsSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
    37	
    38	const OUT = new URL("./xrpl-vectors.json", import.meta.url).pathname;
    39	
    40	const hex = (b: Uint8Array | Buffer) => Buffer.from(b).toString("hex").toUpperCase();
    41	/// An arbitrary fixed message, signed by every key below. Nothing about it is
    42	/// a transaction; it exists to pin what each algorithm hashes.
    43	const PROBE = "5354580012005821000000022200000000";
    44	const rep = (byte: number, n: number) => hex(Buffer.alloc(n, byte));
    45	
    46	// --- 1. seeds -------------------------------------------------------------
    47	// Deterministic entropy so the vectors are stable. These are NOT keys anybody
    48	// should fund: the entropy is a constant published in this file.
    49	// Both algorithms. Devnet's faucet hands out ed25519, so a signer that only
    50	// spoke secp256k1 could not sign for any account this project already has.
    51	const entropies = [0x00, 0x11, 0x7f, 0xff];
    52	const keys = [
    53	  ...entropies.map((b) => ({ b, algo: "secp256k1" as const })),
    54	  ...entropies.map((b) => ({ b, algo: "ed25519" as const })),
    55	].map(({ b, algo }) => {
    56	  const entropy = Buffer.alloc(16, b);
    57	  const seed = encodeSeed(entropy, algo);
    58	  const kp = deriveKeypair(seed);
    59	  const address = deriveAddress(kp.publicKey);
    60	  return {
    61	    seed,
    62	    algorithm: algo,
    63	    entropy: hex(entropy),
    64	    public_key: kp.publicKey.toUpperCase(),
    65	    account_id: hex(decodeAccountID(address)),
    66	    address,
    67	    // The same message signed by both, so the Rust side can pin that
    68	    // secp256k1 signs SHA512Half(message) and ed25519 signs the message.
    69	    signature_over_probe: sign(PROBE, kp.privateKey),
    70	  };
    71	});
    72	
    73	// --- 2. transactions ------------------------------------------------------
    74	// Field values are structurally correct — right lengths, right shapes — but
    75	// they are NOT real proofs. These vectors pin the ENCODER, not the prover;
    76	// `signer/tests/prover_pristine.rs` pins the prover.
    77	const holder = keys[0];
    78	const dest = keys[1];
    79	const ed_holder = keys.find((k) => k.algorithm === "ed25519")!;
    80

    82	  send: {
    83	    TransactionType: "ConfidentialMPTSend",
    84	    Account: holder.address,
    85	    Destination: dest.address,
    86	    MPTokenIssuanceID: rep(0x5a, 24),
    87	    SenderEncryptedAmount: rep(0x01, 66),
    88	    DestinationEncryptedAmount: rep(0x02, 66),

    42	 *   untracked-not-ignored — read from disk. A gitignored file is not scanned,
    43	 *   because it is not published; that is a property, and a control pins it.
    44	 * * **`--rev`:** the blobs of exactly one commit's tree, read from git objects.
    45	 *   ⚠ It scans no history. The publication is two commits, so the phase 1 gate
    46	 *   is NOT this form: it is `--repo <export> --strict-history` on a clean export
    47	 *   (`REVIEW-057` R1-7 and R2-7).
    48	 * * **History:** every blob, commit message and tag message reachable from any
    49	 *   ref, and every path any commit ever touched. ⚠ **Personal paths and emails
    50	 *   in history are REPORTED, not failed**, because the launch plan rules that
    51	 *   history is squashed at publication (`CLAIMS.md` E-309) and this repository
    52	 *   stays the private archive. Secrets fail in history like anywhere else.
    53	 *   `--strict-history` fails on both, and is the form for the export: its two
    54	 *   commits are published history, so nothing in them may be merely reported.
    55	 *
    56	 * **No path is exempt, including `docs/reviews/` and this file.** Exempting a
   598	  ];

   601	// ------------------------------------------------------------- enumeration
   602	
   603	/**
   604	 * ⚠ **Every git call ignores replacement objects** (`REVIEW-056` S2-2). By
   605	 * default `cat-file` and `rev-list` silently substitute any object named under
   606	 * `refs/replace/`, so a local replacement could show this gate harmless bytes
   607	 * while a push publishes the original.
   608	 */
   609	const GIT_ENV: Record<string, string> = { GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS: "1" };
   610	
   611	function git(repo: string, args: string[], input?: Uint8Array, env?: Record<string, string>): Uint8Array {
   612	  const r = Bun.spawnSync(["git", "-C", repo, ...args], { stdin: input, stdout: "pipe", stderr: "pipe", env: { ...process.env, ...GIT_ENV, ...(env ?? {}) } });
   613	  if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`git ${args.join(" ")} failed: ${new TextDecoder().decode(r.stderr).trim()}`);
   614	  return r.stdout;
   615	}
   616	const gitText = (repo: string, args: string[], input?: string) => new TextDecoder().decode(git(repo, args, input === undefined ? undefined : new TextEncoder().encode(input)));
   617	
   618	/** Bytes as latin1, so every byte is one character and nothing is replaced. */
   619	const asText = (b: Uint8Array) => Buffer.from(b.buffer, b.byteOffset, b.byteLength).toString("latin1");
   620	
   621	export interface Unit {
   622	  where: string; text: string; scope: "tree" | "history"; kind: "file" | "message" | "path";
   623	  /** How many published paths carry this content; a file count counts these, not blobs. */
   624	  paths?: number;
   625	}
   626	
   627	function* batchObjects(repo: string, shas: string[]): Generator<{ sha: string; type: string; body: Uint8Array }> {
   628	  if (!shas.length) return;
   629	  const out = git(repo, ["cat-file", "--batch"], new TextEncoder().encode(shas.join("\n") + "\n"));
   630	  let i = 0;
   631	  const dec = new TextDecoder();
   632	  while (i < out.length) {
   633	    const nl = out.indexOf(10, i);
   634	    const [sha, type, size] = dec.decode(out.subarray(i, nl)).split(" ");
   635	    if (type === "missing") throw new Error(`object ${sha} missing`);
   636	    const n = Number(size);
   637	    yield { sha, type, body: out.subarray(nl + 1, nl + 1 + n) };
   638	    i = nl + 1 + n + 1;
   639	  }
   640	}

   640	}
   641	
   642	/**
   643	 * ⚠ **Refuse what this gate cannot read as published** (`REVIEW-056` S2-1, S2-4).
   644	 * A `filter` attribute means `git add` stores bytes a clean filter produced, or,
   645	 * for Git LFS, a pointer while the content is published elsewhere. A
   646	 * `working-tree-encoding` attribute re-encodes on the way in. A gitlink or a
   647	 * nested repository publishes none of its content here. In each case the bytes
   648	 * on disk are not the bytes published, so the gate refuses rather than scanning
   649	 * the wrong ones. `git check-attr` reads `.gitattributes`, `.git/info/attributes`
   650	 * and `core.attributesFile` alike.
   651	 */
   652	function refuseTransformedTree(repo: string, paths: string[], gitlinks: string[], source?: string): void {
   653	  if (gitlinks.length) throw new Error(`a submodule or nested repository at ${gitlinks.slice(0, 5).join(", ")}: its content is another repository and is not scanned`);
   654	  if (!paths.length) return;
   655	  // ⚠ For `--rev`, the attributes are read FROM THAT COMMIT with `--source`, and
   656	  // parsed by git, so a comment that mentions `filter=` is not a filter (R2-6).
   657	  const src = source ? ["--source", source] : [];
   658	  const out = gitText(repo, ["check-attr", "-z", "--stdin", ...src, "filter", "working-tree-encoding"], paths.join("\0") + "\0").split("\0");
   659	  const bad: string[] = [];
   660	  for (let i = 0; i + 2 < out.length; i += 3) if (out[i + 2] !== "unspecified" && out[i + 2] !== "unset") bad.push(`${out[i]} (${out[i + 1]}=${out[i + 2]})`);
   661	  if (bad.length) throw new Error(`content-transforming attributes on ${bad.length} path(s), e.g. ${bad.slice(0, 3).join(", ")}: the published bytes are not the bytes on disk`);
   662	}
   663	
   664	/** What `git add -A` would commit, read from disk. Symlinks are not followed. */
   665	export function treeUnits(repo: string): Unit[] {
   666	  const names = gitText(repo, ["ls-files", "-z", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard"]).split("\0").filter(Boolean);
   667	  const staged = gitText(repo, ["ls-files", "-z", "-s"]).split("\0").filter(Boolean);
   668	  const gitlinks = staged.filter((l) => l.startsWith("160000 ")).map((l) => l.slice(l.indexOf("\t") + 1));
   669	  // An untracked nested repository is listed as a directory: `git add -A` would record a gitlink.
   670	  gitlinks.push(...names.filter((n) => n.endsWith("/")));
   671	  refuseTransformedTree(repo, names.filter((n) => !n.endsWith("/")), gitlinks);
   672	  const units: Unit[] = [];
   673	  for (const n of [...new Set(names)]) {
   674	    const p = join(repo, n);
   675	    units.push({ where: n, text: n, scope: "tree", kind: "path" });
   676	    if (!existsSync(p) && !lstatSync(p, { throwIfNoEntry: false })) {
   677	      // Deleted on disk, still in the index: the index copy is what a plain commit publishes.
   678	      try { units.push({ where: n, text: asText(git(repo, ["cat-file", "blob", `:${n}`])), scope: "tree", kind: "file" }); } catch { /* not in index either */ }
   679	      continue;
   680	    }
   681	    const s = lstatSync(p);
   682	    if (s.isSymbolicLink()) units.push({ where: `${n} (symlink target)`, text: readlinkSync(p), scope: "tree", kind: "file" });
   683	    else if (s.isFile()) units.push({ where: n, text: asText(readFileSync(p)), scope: "tree", kind: "file" });
   684	  }
   685	  return units;
   686	}

     1	# REVIEW-057 round two — self notes, written BEFORE the leg ran
     2	
     3	Subject frozen at 776ca25.
     4	
     5	T1. The guard records the history's roots at install time. A root added later —
     6	    an unrelated history merged in, or the history rewritten onto a new root —
     7	    is unknown to it, and pushes of that history are unguarded until the
     8	    installer is re-run. Nothing says so.
     9	T2. The guard trusts `gh` to identify the destination. `GH_HOST` or an
    10	    enterprise token in the environment would send the lookup elsewhere. And
    11	    it is unverified whether `gh api repos/<old-name>` follows the redirect
    12	    after the rename; if it does not, a push to the old URL is refused (the
    13	    safe direction), but the plan does not say which.
    14	T3. The inventory matches code line by line after collapsing whitespace. A
    15	    quotation with line-number prefixes (`cat -n`, `nl`, `123:` from grep -n),
    16	    or reflowed by a formatter, evades it. Review transcripts are full of
    17	    `cat -n` and `grep -n` output. Likely false negatives in exactly the files
    18	    that quote most.
    19	T4. The inventory compares rippled at 3.3.0 and develop only; records also
    20	    read 3.4.x and other tags, and mpt-crypto at 1.0.5. A quotation of code
    21	    that exists only in another version is missed.
    22	T5. An orphan commit made in a private clone from the private working tree is
    23	    not "private history" by the guard's definition, so the guard allows
    24	    pushing it anywhere, without the step 7 gate having run on it.

   722	export function historyUnits(repo: string): Unit[] {
   723	  // ⚠ A shallow clone or a grafts file hides history from rev-list, so a pass
   724	  // over it would not be a pass over what the repository holds.
   725	  if (gitText(repo, ["rev-parse", "--is-shallow-repository"]).trim() === "true") throw new Error("the repository is shallow; history mode needs every commit");
   726	  const grafts = gitText(repo, ["rev-parse", "--git-path", "info/grafts"]).trim();
   727	  if (existsSync(join(repo, grafts)) || existsSync(grafts)) throw new Error(`a grafts file exists at ${grafts}; it rewrites what rev-list sees`);
   728	  const lines = gitText(repo, ["rev-list", "--objects", "--all"]).split("\n").filter(Boolean);
   729	  const pathOf = new Map<string, string>();
   730	  for (const l of lines) {
   731	    const sp = l.indexOf(" ");
   732	    const sha = sp === -1 ? l : l.slice(0, sp);
   733	    if (!pathOf.has(sha)) pathOf.set(sha, sp === -1 ? "" : l.slice(sp + 1));
   734	  }
   735	  const tags = gitText(repo, ["for-each-ref", "--format=%(objectname) %(objecttype)", "refs/tags"]).split("\n").filter((l) => l.endsWith(" tag")).map((l) => l.split(" ")[0]);
   736	  const units: Unit[] = [];
   737	  for (const o of batchObjects(repo, [...pathOf.keys(), ...tags])) {
   738	    const p = pathOf.get(o.sha) ?? "";
   739	    if (o.type === "blob") units.push({ where: `${p} @ blob ${o.sha.slice(0, 10)}`, text: asText(o.body), scope: "history", kind: "file" });
   740	    else if (o.type === "commit" || o.type === "tag") units.push({ where: `${o.type} ${o.sha.slice(0, 10)} message`, text: messageOf(o.body), scope: "history", kind: "message" });
   741	  }
   742	  // NUL-separated, because a path may contain a tab or a newline (REVIEW-056 S2-3).
   743	  const paths = new Set(gitText(repo, ["log", "--all", "-m", "--format=", "--name-only", "--no-renames", "-z"]).split("\0").filter(Boolean));
   744	  for (const p of paths) units.push({ where: `${p} (a path some commit touched)`, text: p, scope: "history", kind: "path" });
   745	  return units;
   746	}
   747	
   748	export interface Finding extends Hit { where: string; line: number; scope: "tree" | "history" }

   710	/** A commit or tag message, without the identity and signature headers. */
   711	function messageOf(body: Uint8Array): string {
   712	  const t = asText(body);
   713	  const split = t.indexOf("\n\n");
   714	  const head = split === -1 ? t : t.slice(0, split);
   715	  const msg = split === -1 ? "" : t.slice(split + 2);
   716	  // Keep header lines that are not identity or signature: none carry free text
   717	  // except `encoding`, so only the message is returned.
   718	  void head;
   719	  return msg;
   720	}

/bin/zsh -lc "cat > .review-r3/probe-inventory.py <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess, os, hashlib, re, shutil
r=Path.cwd();p=r/'.review-r3/inventory-probes';p.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
a=subprocess.Popen(['git','archive','a36f165'],stdout=subprocess.PIPE);subprocess.run(['tar','-x','-C',str(p)],stdin=a.stdout,check=True);a.stdout.close();a.wait();subprocess.run(['git','init','-q',str(p)],check=True)
(p/'node_modules').symlink_to(r/'node_modules',target_is_directory=True)
q=p/'docs/r3-probes';q.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
meta=[]
def put(name,body,src):

Define a word that will expand to a full gh command when invoked.

The expansion may specify additional arguments and flags. If the expansion includes
positional placeholders such as `$1`, extra arguments that follow the alias will be
inserted appropriately. Otherwise, extra arguments will be appended to the expanded
command.

Use `-` as expansion argument to read the expansion string from standard input. This
is useful to avoid quoting issues when defining expansions.

If the expansion starts with `!` or if `--shell` was given, the expansion is a shell
expression that will be evaluated through the `sh` interpreter when the alias is
invoked. This allows for chaining multiple commands via piping and redirection.

import os,subprocess,socket,threading,json

def serve():
 try:
  for _ in range(2):
   c,_=s.accept();b=c.recv(16384);requests.append(b.split(b'\\r\\n',1)[0].decode(errors='replace')[:150])

codex
The inventory attacks reproduced two gaps. It missed a 94,993-byte rippled test-vector quotation after the lines were joined, and missed reflowed React and Elysia manifests. It also passed a licence file stored as a symlink into ignored `node_modules`; the exported archive contained a dangling link rather than the notice bytes.

The real `gh` binary exposed another host-pinning gap: `--hostname github.com` still honoured `http_unix_socket` from a custom `GH_CONFIG_DIR`. A local server supplied the archive ID, and the unmodified hook accepted it for a different repository URL.

   282	 * include blocks and licence text. A held set naming both counts for rippled
   283	 * only.
   284	 */
   285	const DERIVED_FROM: Record<string, string> = { "XRPLF/clio": "XRPLF/rippled" };
   286	function provenance(held: Set<string>): Set<string> {
   287	  const out = new Set(held);
   288	  for (const [derived, origin] of Object.entries(DERIVED_FROM)) if (out.has(derived) && out.has(origin)) out.delete(derived);
   289	  return out;
   290	}
   291	const GENERIC_PROJECTS = 3;
   292	const LICENCE_SHARE = 0.8;
   293	
   294	function tracked(): string[] {
   295	  return run(["git", "ls-files"], ROOT).split("\n").filter((f) =>
   296	    f && TEXT.test(f) && !/(^|\/)(bun\.lock|Cargo\.lock|package-lock\.json)$/.test(f)
   297	    && f !== "LICENSE" && f !== "NOTICE" && !f.startsWith("LICENSES/")
   298	    && !f.startsWith("docs/third-party/") && existsSync(join(ROOT, f)));
   299	}
   300	
   301	/**
   302	 * Code runs: consecutive substantive lines, with gaps of up to MAX_GAP, each
   303	 * found in at least one corpus. A run is attributed to every project holding
   304	 * MIN_RUN or more of its lines; it is LICENCE TEXT when most of its lines come
   305	 * from licence files, and SHARED BOILERPLATE when more than GENERIC_PROJECTS
   306	 * projects hold it (Cargo's generated header is in every crate).
   307	 */
   308	function codeRuns(file: string, raw: string[]): { attributed: Run[]; licence: Run[]; generic: Run[] } {
   309	  const isPatch = file.endsWith(".patch");
   310	  const lines = raw.map((l) => norm(stripPrefix(isPatch && /^[ +-]/.test(l) && !/^(\+\+\+|---) /.test(l) ? l.slice(1) : l)));
   311	  const out = { attributed: [] as Run[], licence: [] as Run[], generic: [] as Run[] };
   312	  let run: number[] = [];
   313	  let gap = 0;
   314	  const close = () => {
   315	    if (run.length >= MIN_RUN) {
   316	      // Only SPECIFIC lines — held by GENERIC_PROJECTS projects or fewer —
   317	      // count toward attribution. Counting every line let a few shared
   318	      // attributes in a long quotation push the whole of it into boilerplate,
   319	      // and under-attributing is the unsafe direction for a notice.
   320	      const specific = new Map<string, number>();
   321	      const any = new Set<string>();
   322	      let lic = 0;
   323	      for (const i of run) {
   324	        const held = provenance(LINES.get(lines[i])!);
   325	        for (const p of held) any.add(p);
   326	        if (held.size <= GENERIC_PROJECTS) for (const p of held) specific.set(p, (specific.get(p) ?? 0) + 1);
   327	        if (LICENCE_LINES.has(lines[i])) lic++;
   328	      }
   329	      const projects = [...specific].filter(([, k]) => k >= MIN_RUN).map(([p]) => p).sort();
   330	      const r: Run = { file, from: run[0] + 1, to: run[run.length - 1] + 1, kind: "code", projects: projects.length ? projects : [...any].sort(), lines: run.length };
   331	      if (lic / run.length >= LICENCE_SHARE) out.licence.push(r);
   332	      else if (projects.length) out.attributed.push(r);
   333	      else if (any.size > GENERIC_PROJECTS) out.generic.push(r);
   334	    }
   335	    run = [];
   336	    gap = 0;
   337	  };
   338	  lines.forEach((l, i) => {
   339	    if (!substantive(l)) { if (run.length && ++gap > MAX_GAP) close(); return; }
   340	    if (LINES.has(l)) { run.push(i); gap = 0; } else close();
   341	  });
   342	  close();
   343	  return out;
   344	}
   529	/**
   530	 * The NOTICE check, as a manifest rather than a name search (`REVIEW-057`
   531	 * R2-6, where a NOTICE of six bare names and a deleted licence file passed).
   532	 * For every project found: NOTICE must name it in a bullet; a project with a
   533	 * licence must name a LICENSES/ file in that bullet, and the file must exist
   534	 * and be byte-identical to one of the project's own root licence files; a
   535	 * project with none must be stated as carrying no licence.
   536	 */
   537	function noticeProblems(): string[] {
   538	  const path = join(ROOT, "NOTICE");
   539	  if (!existsSync(path)) return ["NOTICE does not exist"];
   540	  const notice = readFileSync(path, "utf8");
   541	  const problems: string[] = staleAside.map((e) => `set-aside entry for ${e.project} in ${e.file} ${e.from}–${e.to} matches no hit; delete it`);
   542	  for (const p of found) {
   543	    const name = p.replace(/^(crate|npm) /, "");
   544	    const at = notice.indexOf(name);
   545	    if (at < 0) { problems.push(`NOTICE does not name ${name}`); continue; }
   546	    const start = notice.lastIndexOf("\n", at) + 1;
   547	    const next = notice.slice(at).search(/\n\s*(-|\d+\.) |\n\n/);
   548	    const entry = notice.slice(start, next < 0 ? undefined : at + next);
   549	    const lic = LICENCE_OF.get(p) ?? "";
   550	    if (/no licence/i.test(lic)) {
   551	      if (!/no licence/i.test(entry)) problems.push(`NOTICE's entry for ${name} does not say it carries no licence`);
   552	      continue;
   553	    }
   554	    const file = entry.match(/LICENSES\/[\w.-]+/)?.[0];
   555	    if (!file) { problems.push(`NOTICE's entry for ${name} names no LICENSES/ file`); continue; }
   556	    if (!existsSync(join(ROOT, file))) { problems.push(`${file}, named for ${name}, does not exist`); continue; }
   557	    const ours = readFileSync(join(ROOT, file));
   558	    if (!(LICENCE_FILES.get(p) ?? []).some((b) => b.equals(ours))) {
   559	      problems.push(`${file} is not byte-identical to any root licence file of ${name}`);
   560	    }
   561	  }
   562	  return problems;
   563	}
   564	
   565	if (process.argv.includes("--check")) {
   566	  const problems = noticeProblems();
   567	  if (problems.length) {
   568	    for (const x of problems) console.log(`check: ${x}`);
   569	    console.log(`check: FAIL — ${problems.length} problem(s)`);
   570	    process.exit(1);
   571	  }
   572	  console.log(`check: PASS — every one of ${found.length} projects found has a NOTICE entry, and each licensed one a verbatim LICENSES/ file`);
   573	}

   186	function tokens(text: string): { tok: string[]; line: number[] } {
   187	  const tok: string[] = [], line: number[] = [];
   188	  text.split("\n").forEach((l, i) => {
   189	    for (const m of l.matchAll(/[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*|\d\w*/g)) { tok.push(m[0]); line.push(i); }
   190	  });
   191	  return { tok, line };
   192	}
   193	
   194	/** A window of mostly names, not numbers: a table of published constants is no quotation. */
   195	function windowValid(tok: string[], i: number): boolean {
   196	  let names = 0;
   197	  for (let k = i; k < i + TOKEN_WINDOW; k++) if (/^[A-Za-z_]/.test(tok[k])) names++;
   198	  return names * 2 >= TOKEN_WINDOW;
   199	}
   200	const MANIFEST = /(^|\/)(Cargo\.toml(\.orig)?|package\.json|[^/]*\.lock)$/;
   244	    // Licence texts are left to the line pass, which classifies them; minified
   245	    // files carry no quotable structure and cost most of the time.
   246	    // Minified files carry no quotable structure and cost most of the time;
   247	    // manifests are dependency lists every crate shares, and are left to the
   248	    // line pass.
   249	    if (!/^.{2000,}$/m.test(text) && !MANIFEST.test(f)) {
   250	      const { tok } = tokens(text);
   251	      for (let i = 0; i + TOKEN_WINDOW <= tok.length; i++) {
   252	        const h = windowHash(tok, i);
   253	        if (!TRACKED_HASHES.has(h)) continue;
   254	        if (isLicence) { LICENCE_WINDOWS.add(h); continue; }
   255	        let set = TOKEN_HITS.get(h);
   256	        if (!set) TOKEN_HITS.set(h, (set = new Set()));
   257	        set.add(project(c));
   258	      }
   259	    }

  1172	export function parseArgs(argv: string[]): { flags: Set<string>; repo?: string; rev?: string; stores: string[] } {
  1173	  const FLAGS = new Set(["--no-local-store", "--history-only", "--tree-only", "--strict-history", "--controls-only"]);
  1174	  const OPTS = new Set(["--repo", "--rev", "--store"]);
  1175	  const flags = new Set<string>();
  1176	  const out: { flags: Set<string>; repo?: string; rev?: string; stores: string[] } = { flags, stores: [] };
  1177	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
  1178	    const a = argv[i];
  1179	    if (FLAGS.has(a)) { flags.add(a); continue; }
  1180	    if (!OPTS.has(a)) throw new Error(`unknown argument '${a}'`);
  1181	    const v = argv[++i];
  1182	    if (v === undefined || v.startsWith("--")) throw new Error(`${a} needs a value`);
  1183	    if (a === "--store") out.stores.push(v);
  1184	    else if (a === "--repo") out.repo = v;
  1185	    else out.rev = v;
  1186	  }

  1188	  if (out.rev && flags.has("--history-only")) throw new Error("--rev scans one tree; it cannot be combined with --history-only");
  1189	  if (flags.has("--strict-history") && (out.rev || flags.has("--tree-only"))) throw new Error("--strict-history has no effect without a history scan");

  1191	  return out;

- **R3-1 | HIGH | A remote named `--parse-url` bypasses destination authorization | `tools/git-hooks/pre-push:70–72` | Give parser mode an invocation that cannot overlap Git’s two hook arguments, and add this remote-name case.**

- **R3-2 | HIGH | `--hostname github.com` does not pin the API connection | `tools/git-hooks/pre-push:91–95`; `tools/install-push-guard.sh:37` | Isolate or reject API routing overrides, including `http_unix_socket` and `api_host`, before trusting the response.**

  Using the **real `gh` binary**, a custom `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, a synthetic token and a local Unix-socket HTTP server, `gh api --hostname github.com repos/pub/lic` returned the locally supplied `100 true`. The unmodified hook then accepted that different repository as archive ID 100. The local server recorded both requests. GitHub documents these routing settings in its [CLI configuration manual](https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_config).

- **R3-3 | HIGH | The hook authenticates the URL’s repository, not the receiving transport | `tools/git-hooks/pre-push:22–24,87–102` | Constrain permitted transports and reject routing overrides that prevent establishing the actual destination.**

  Both `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` and `core.sshCommand` redirected an archive-looking SSH URL to another local bare repository. The hook accepted the archive ID; the other receiver obtained the private fixture. SSH host configuration and transport commands therefore remain outside the claimed property.

- **R3-4 | HIGH | Installation from a subdirectory reports success but installs outside the clone’s effective hooks directory | `tools/install-push-guard.sh:25–27,46–48` | Resolve the hooks path absolutely, or resolve its relative result against the invoking directory.**

  From `private/tools`, Git returned `../.git/hooks`. The installer prepended the repository root, producing `private/../.git/hooks/pre-push`. It exited 0 while `private/.git/hooks/pre-push` was absent. A subsequent direct push to a local bare repository succeeded and delivered the private fixture.

- **R3-5 | HIGH | Reinstallation can replace the trusted archive ID with a stale push URL’s repository ID | `tools/install-push-guard.sh:32–43`; D:360–363 | Preserve the established archive ID across reinstalls and validate every push URL; explicitly reconcile `pushurl` in step 8.**

  I started with archive ID 100, set a stale `remote.origin.pushurl` to repository 200, and executed step 8’s `git remote set-url origin` correction. A newly created publication repository is still private at this point, so the visibility check does not prevent this mistake.
